Gauge-invariant TMD factorization for Drell-Yan hadronic tensor at small 𝒙 x Preprint: JLAB-THY-20-3294
I. Balitsky

Abstract
The Drell-Yan hadronic tensor for electromagnetic (EM) current is calculated in the Sudakov region s ≫ Q 2 ≫ q ⟂ 2 s\gg Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2} with 1 Q 2 {1\over Q^{2}} accuracy, first at the tree level and then with the double-log accuracy. It is demonstrated that in the leading order in N c N_{c} the higher-twist quark-quark-gluon TMDs reduce to leading-twist TMDs due to QCD equation of motion. The resulting tensor for unpolarized hadrons is EM gauge-invariant and depends on two leading-twist TMDs: f 1 f_{1} responsible for total DY cross section, and Boer-Mulders function h 1 ⟂ h_{1}^{\perp} . The order-of-magnitude estimates of angular distributions for DY process seem to agree with LHC results at corresponding kinematics.



8

1
Introduction

The Drell-Yan (DY) process of production of lepton pairs with large invariant
mass in hadronic collisions
[
1
]
is one of the most important tools to study QCD. From
experimental viewpoint, it is
a unique source of information
about partonic structure of hadrons
[
2
]
. On the theoretical side, it serves as a testing ground for
factorization approaches in various kinematics regions, like the classical collinear factorization
[
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
]
, TMD factorization

[
9
,
10
,
11
,
12
,
13
]
, and
SCET
[
14
,
15
,
16
,
17
]
.

The differential cross section of DY process is determined by the product of leptonic tensor and hadronic tensor.
The hadronic tensor
W_{\mu\nu}
is defined as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(p_{A},p_{B},q)~

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}

\displaystyle~{1\over(2\pi)^{4}}\sum_{X}\!\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|J_{\mu}(x)|X\rangle\langle X|J_{\nu}(0)|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle

(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{1\over(2\pi)^{4}}\!\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|J_{\mu}(x)J_{\nu}(0)|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle.

where
p_{A},p_{B}
are hadron momenta,
q
is the momentum of DY pair,
\sum_{X}
denotes the sum over full set of “out” states and
J_{\mu}
is either electromagnetic or
Z
-boson current. In this paper I consider only the case of electromagnetic current, the Z-boson case
will be studied in a separate publication.
For unpolarized hadrons, the hadronic tensor
W_{\mu\nu}
is parametrized by 4 functions, for example in Collins-Soper frame
[
18
]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}~=~-\big(g_{\mu\nu}-{q_{\mu}q_{\nu}\over q^{2}}\big)(W_{T}+W_{{\Delta\Delta}})-2X_{\mu}X_{\nu}W_{\Delta\Delta}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~Z_{\mu}Z_{\nu}(W_{L}-W_{T}-W_{\Delta\Delta})-(X_{\mu}Z_{\nu}+X_{\nu}Z_{\mu})W_{\Delta}

(2)

where
X
,
Z
are unit vectors orthogonal to
q
and to each other (their explicit form is presented in Sect.
8.2
).

Conventionally, the analysis of hadronic tensor (
1
) in the Sudakov region
q^{2}\equiv Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
is performed by
using TMD factorization. For example, functions
W_{T}
and
W_{\Delta\Delta}
can be represented in a standard
TMD-factorized way
[
9
,
19
]

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054ptW_{i}~=~\sum_{\rm flavors}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}{\cal D}_{f/A}^{(i)}(x_{A},k_{\perp}){\cal D}_{f/B}^{(i)}(x_{B},q_{\perp}-k_{\perp})C_{i}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt+~{\rm power~corrections}~+~{\rm Y-terms}

(3)

where
{\cal D}_{f/A}(x_{A},k_{\perp})
is the TMD density of a parton
f
in hadron
A

with fraction of momentum
x_{A}
and transverse momentum
k_{\perp}
,
{\cal D}_{f/B}(x_{B},q_{\perp}-k_{\perp})
is a similar quantity for hadron
B
, and
coefficient functions
C_{i}(q,k)
are determined by the cross section
\sigma(ff\rightarrow\mu^{+}\mu^{-})
of production of DY pair of invariant mass
q^{2}
in the scattering of two partons.

There is, however, a problem with Eq. (
3
) for the functions
W_{L}
and
W_{\Delta}
.
The reason is that while
W_{T}
and
W_{\Delta\Delta}
are determined by
leading-twist quark TMDs,
W_{L}
and
W_{\Delta}
start from terms
{q_{\perp}\over Q}
and
\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
determined
by quark-quark-gluon TMDs. The power corrections

\sim{q_{\perp}\over Q}
were found in Ref.
[
20
]
more than two decades ago but there was no calculation of power corrections

\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
until recently. Also, the leading-twist contribution is not gauge invariant.

1
1

1

Hereafter gauge invariance of hadronic tensor means electromagnetic (EM) gauge invariance, namely
that
q^{\mu}W_{\mu\nu}~=~0
.

It is well known from DVCS studies that check of EM gauge invariance sometimes involves
cancellation of contributions of different twists (see e.g.
[
21
,
22
,
23
,
24
,
25
,
26
,
27
]
) so the fact that we need power corrections to check
q^{\mu}W_{\mu\nu}~=~0

should not come as a surprise. Still, the absence of gauge invariance may cause discomfort in practical applications of TMD factorization.

In a recent paper
[
28
]
A. Tarasov and the author calculated power corrections
\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
to total DY cross section production which are determined by quark-quark-gluon operators. In this paper I present the result of calculation of symmetric part of
W_{\mu\nu}(q)
for unpolarized hadrons at large
s\gg Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
relevant for DY experiments at LHC. The method of calculation is based on the rapidity factorization approach developed in
Refs.
[
29
,
28
]
.
The calculations will be performed in the leading order in perturbation theory, first at the tree level and then in the double-logarithmic
approximation for coefficient functions
C_{i}(q,k)
. In this paper I consider
only the production of leptons by virtual photon and leave the case of Z-boson production for future publication.

To find all functions in Eq. (
2
) we need to have gauge-invariant expression for
W_{\mu\nu}
in terms of TMDs.
As noted above, only
W_{T}
and
W_{\Delta\Delta}
come
from leading-twist quark-antiquark TMD while two other structures come from higher-twist quark-antiquark-gluon TMDs.
Fortunately, in the leading
order in
N_{c}
the latter are related to the former by QCD equations of motion (
[
28
]
, see also Ref.
[
20
]
).
Moreover, in the small-
x
region

x_{A},x_{B}\ll 1
all structures can be expressed by just two leading-twist TMDs -
f_{1}(x,k_{\perp})
(responsible for the total cross section) and
h_{1}^{\perp}(x,k_{\perp})
(the Boer-Mulders function
[
30
]
). The results for four functions in Eq. (
2
), presented in next Section, are of the type of Eq. (
3
) with TMDs
f_{1}(x,k_{\perp})
and/or
h_{1}^{\perp}(x,k_{\perp})
and tree-level coefficient functions constructed of
q
and
k_{\perp}
.

The paper is organized as follows.
In section
2
I present the resulting gauge-invariant expression for
W_{\mu\nu}

up to
{1\over Q^{2}}
terms which is calculated in the rest of the paper.
In section
3
the TMD factorization is derived
from the rapidity factorization of the double functional integral for a cross section of particle production.
In section
4
I explain the method of
calculation of power corrections based on approximate solution of classical Yang-Mills equations.
Using this method, DY hadronic tensor for small
x
is calculated in Sections
5
,
6
, and
7
.
Section
8
contains results of calculations and order-of-magnitude estimate of angular coefficients of DY cross section.
The matching of obtained TMDs and coefficient functions
C_{i}
in the double-log approximation is discussed in Sect.
9

and the last section
10
is devoted to conclusions and outlook.
The necessary technical details can be found in appendices.

2
Gauge-invariant hadronic tensor

To set up the stage, in this Section I present the final result for tree-level DY hadronic tensor. It is determined by two leading-twist TMDs:
the function
f_{1}^{f}(x,k_{\perp})
responsible for the total DY cross section and Boer-Mulders time-odd function
h^{\perp}_{1}(x,k_{\perp})

(the explicit definition of these functions is presented in the Appendix
11.2
).
The result reads

\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{1\over N_{c}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big[F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})W_{\mu\nu}^{F}(q,k_{\perp})+H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})W_{\mu\nu}^{H}(q,k_{\perp})\Big]

(4)

where
e_{f}
are electric charges of quarks,
q=x_{A}p_{A}+x_{B}p_{B}+q_{\perp}
and

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802ptF^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~=~f_{1}^{f}\big(x_{A},k_{\perp}\big){\bar{f}}_{1}^{f}\big(x_{B},(q-k)_{\perp}\big)~+~f_{1}^{f}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}_{1}^{f}

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802ptH^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~=~h^{\perp}_{1}\big(x_{A},k_{\perp}\big){\bar{h}}^{\perp}_{1}\big(x_{B},(q-k)_{\perp}\big)~+~h_{1f}^{\perp}\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}_{1f}^{\perp}

(5)

The gauge-invariant structures

W^{F}_{\mu\nu}
and
W^{H}_{\mu\nu}
are given by

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802ptW^{F}_{\mu\nu}(q,k_{\perp})~=~-g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}+{2(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over Q^{2}}g_{\mu\nu}^{\parallel}+{2\over Q^{2}}\big[x_{A}p_{A\mu}k^{\perp}_{\nu}+x_{B}p_{B\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt+{4x_{A}^{2}p_{A\mu}p_{A\nu}\over Q^{4}}k_{\perp}^{2}+{4x_{B}^{2}p_{B\mu}p_{B\nu}\over Q^{4}}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2},

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802ptm^{2}W^{H}_{\mu\nu}(q,k_{\perp})~=~-\big[k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+k^{\perp}_{\nu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\mu}+g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}\big]-2{g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over Q^{2}}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt-~2x_{A}\big[p_{A\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big]{k_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}-2x_{B}\big[p_{B\mu}k^{\perp}_{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big]{(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt-~{4x_{A}^{2}p_{A\mu}p_{A\nu}\over Q^{4}}k_{\perp}^{2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}-{4x_{B}^{2}p_{B\mu}p_{B\nu}\over Q^{4}}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}\Big\}

(6)

where
g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}
and
g_{\mu\nu}^{\parallel}
are transverse and longitudinal parts of metric tensor (the explicit form of our notations is specified in the next Section, see the paragraph including Eq. (
8
)).
It is easy to check that
q^{\mu}W^{F}_{\mu\nu}~=~0
and
q^{\mu}W^{H}_{\mu\nu}~=~0
. As we will see below, in some of the structures the corrections to Eq. (
4
) are of order
O(x_{A})
and
O(x_{B})
while in others on the top of that
there are corrections
\sim O\big({1\over N_{c}}\big)
times some other higher-twist TMDs discussed in Ref.
[
28
]
.
It should be also noted that
W^{F}
part coincides with the result obtained in Refs.
[
31
,
32
]
using
parton Reggeization approach to DY process
[
33
]
.

In the rest of the paper I will derive the above equations and discuss their accuracy. Let me mention upfront that since the approximations made in Eq. (
4
) are
x_{A},x_{B}\ll 1

and
q_{\perp}^{2}\ll Q^{2}\simeq x_{A}x_{B}s
, I
hope that the results of this paper can be used for studies of DY process at LHC with
Q^{2}\sim
100GeV or less.

2
2

2

The reader should not be confused by using small-
x
approximation at LHC with
Q\sim 100
GeV. One should distinguish between small-
x
approximation
and small-
x
resummation.
In the kinematics discussed in this paper
x_{A}\sim x_{B}\sim 0.1
so the small-
x
resummation of

\alpha_{s}\ln x_{A(B)}
is unnecessary, or better to say, should be done on the par with Sudakov resummation of
\alpha_{s}\ln{Q^{2}/q_{\perp}^{2}}
, see e.g. Ref.
[
34
]
. On the other hand, if one has an expression like
f_{1}(x_{A},k_{\perp})+x_{A}f^{\perp}(x_{A},k_{\perp})
, one can safely neglect the second term, see the discussion in Appendix
11.3
.

Last but not least, the derivation of
the above equations is lengthly so the readers interested in final formulas for structures
W_{i}
and the discussion of approximations can go directly to Sect.
8
.

3
TMD factorization from rapidity factorization

As was mentioned in the Introduction, to find the TMD formulas of Eq. (
3
) type
I use the rapidity factorization approach to developed in Refs.
[
29
,
28
]
.
Let me quickly summarize basic ideas of this approach.
The sum over full set of “out” states in Eq. (
1
) can be represented by a double functional integral

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt(2\pi)^{4}W_{\mu\nu}(p_{A},p_{B},q)~=~\sum_{X}\!\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|J_{\mu}(x)|X\rangle\langle X|J_{\nu}(0)|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle

(7)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt=~\lim_{t_{i}\rightarrow-\infty}^{t_{f}\rightarrow\infty}\!\!\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}\!\int^{{\tilde{A}}(t_{f})=A(t_{f})}\!\!D{\tilde{A}}_{\mu}DA_{\mu}\!\int^{\tilde{\psi}(t_{f})=\psi(t_{f})}\!D\tilde{\bar{\psi}}D\tilde{\psi}D{\bar{\psi}}D\psi~\Psi^{\ast}_{p_{A}}(\vec{{\tilde{A}}}(t_{i}),\tilde{\psi}(t_{i}))

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\times~\Psi^{\ast}_{p_{B}}(\vec{{\tilde{A}}}(t_{i}),\tilde{\psi}(t_{i}))e^{-iS_{\rm QCD}({\tilde{A}},\tilde{\psi})}e^{iS_{\rm QCD}(A,\psi)}\tilde{J}_{\mu}(x)J_{\nu}(0)\Psi_{p_{A}}(\vec{A}(t_{i}),\psi(t_{i}))\Psi_{p_{B}}(\vec{A}(t_{i}),\psi(t_{i})).

where
J_{\mu}=\sum_{\rm flavors}e_{f}{\bar{\psi}}_{f}\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{f}
is the electromagnetic current.
In this double functional integral the amplitude
\langle X|J_{\mu}(0)|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle
is given by the integral over
\psi,A
fields
whereas the complex conjugate amplitude
\langle p_{A},p_{B}|J^{\mu}(x)|X\rangle
is represented by the integral over
\tilde{\psi},{\tilde{A}}
fields.
Also,
\Psi_{p}(\vec{A}(t_{i}),\psi(t_{i}))
denotes the proton wave function at the initial time
t_{i}
and the boundary conditions

{\tilde{A}}(t_{f})=A(t_{f})
and
\tilde{\psi}(t_{f})=\psi(t_{f})
reflect the sum over all states
X
, cf. Refs.
[
35
,
36
,
37
]
.

We use
Sudakov variables
p=\alpha p_{1}+\beta p_{2}+p_{\perp}
, where
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are light-like vectors close to
p_{A}
and
p_{B}
so that

p_{A}=p_{1}+{m^{2}\over s}p_{2}
and
p_{A}=p_{1}+{m^{2}\over s}p_{2}
with
m
being the proton mass.
Also, we use the notations
x_{\bullet}\equiv x_{\mu}p_{1}^{\mu}
and
x_{\ast}\equiv x_{\mu}p_{2}^{\mu}

for the dimensionless light-cone coordinates (
x_{\ast}=\sqrt{s\over 2}x_{+}
and
x_{\bullet}=\sqrt{s\over 2}x_{-}
). Our metric is
g^{\mu\nu}~=~(1,-1,-1,-1)

which we will frequently rewrite as a sum of longitudinal part and transverse part:

g^{\mu\nu}~=~g_{\parallel}^{\mu\nu}+g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp}~=~{2\over s}\big(p_{1}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}+p_{2}^{\mu}p_{1}^{\nu})+g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}

(8)

Consequently,
p\cdot q~=~(\alpha_{p}\beta_{q}+\alpha_{q}\beta_{p}){s\over 2}-(p,q)_{\perp}
where
(p,q)_{\perp}\equiv-p_{i}q^{i}
.
Throughout the paper, the sum over the Latin indices
i
,
j
, … runs over two transverse components while the sum over Greek indices
\mu
,
\nu
, … runs over four components as usual.

Following Ref.
[
29
]
we separate quark and gluon fields in the functional integral (
7
) into three sectors (see figure
1
):
“projectile” fields
A_{\mu},\psi_{A}

with
|\beta|<\sigma_{p}
,
“target” fields
B_{\mu},\psi_{B}
with
|\alpha|<\sigma_{t}
and “central rapidity” fields
C_{\mu},\psi_{C}
with
|\alpha|>\sigma_{t}
and
|\beta|>\sigma_{p}
,
see Fig.
1
.

3
3

3

Although the kinematics is best suited for LHC collider, I call
A
hadron “projectile” and
B
hadron
“target” for convenience.

Figure 1:
Rapidity factorization for DY particle production

Our goal is to integrate over central fields and get
the amplitude in the factorized form, i.e. as a product of functional integrals over
A
fields representing projectile matrix elements (TMDs of the projectile)
and functional integrals over
B
fields representing target matrix elements (TMDs of the target).
In the spirit of background-field method, we “freeze” projectile and target fields and get a sum of diagrams in these external fields.
Since
|\beta|<\sigma_{p}
in the projectile fields and
|\alpha|<\sigma_{t}
in the target fields, at the tree level
one can set with power accuracy
\beta=0
for the projectile fields and
\alpha=0
for the target fields - the corrections will
be
O\big({m^{2}\over\sigma_{p}s}\big)
and
O\big({m^{2}\over\sigma_{t}s}\big)
.
Beyond the tree level, the integration over
C
fields produces
logarithms of the cutoffs
\sigma_{p}
and
\sigma_{t}
which match the corresponding
logs in TMDs of the projectile and the target, see the discussion in Sect.
9

From integrals over projectile and target fields in the above equation we see that
the functional integral over
C
fields should
be done in the background of
A
and
B
fields satisfying

{\tilde{A}}(t_{f})~=~A(t_{f}),~~~\tilde{\psi}_{A}(t_{f})~=~\psi_{A}(t_{f})~~{\rm and}~~~{\tilde{B}}(t_{f})~=~B(t_{f}),~~~\tilde{\psi}_{B}(t_{f})~=~\psi_{B}(t_{f}).

(9)

Combining this with our approximation
that at the tree level
\beta=0
for
A
,
{\tilde{A}}
fields and
\alpha=0
for
B
,
{\tilde{B}}
fields,
which corresponds to
A=A(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}),~{\tilde{A}}={\tilde{A}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})
and
B=B(x_{\ast},x_{\perp}),~{\tilde{B}}={\tilde{B}}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})
,
we see that for the purpose of calculation of the functional integral over central fields we can set

\displaystyle A(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})={\tilde{A}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}),~~~~\psi_{A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})=\tilde{\psi}_{A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})

\displaystyle{\rm and}

\displaystyle B(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})={\tilde{B}}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp}),~~~~\psi_{B}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})=\tilde{\psi}_{B}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp}).

(10)

In other words, since
A
,
\psi
and
{\tilde{A}}
,
\tilde{\psi}
do not depend on
x_{\ast}
, if they coincide at
x_{\ast}=\infty
they coincide everywhere.
Similarly, since
B
,
\psi_{B}
and
{\tilde{B}}
,
\tilde{\psi}_{B}
do not depend on
x_{\bullet}
, if they coincide at
x_{\bullet}=\infty
they should be equal.

Summarizing, we see that at the tree level in our approximation

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\int\!DC_{\mu}\!\int^{{\tilde{C}}(t_{f})=C(t_{f})}D{\tilde{C}}_{\mu}\!\int\!D{\bar{\psi}}_{C}D\psi_{C}\int^{\tilde{\psi}_{C}(t_{f})=\psi_{C}(t_{f})}~D\tilde{\bar{\psi}}_{C}D\tilde{\psi}_{C}~{\tilde{J}}_{\mu}(x)J_{\nu}(0)~e^{-i{\tilde{S}}_{C}+iS_{C}}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{\cal O}(q,x;A,\psi_{A};B,\psi_{B}),

(11)

where now
S_{C}~=~S_{\rm QCD}(C+A+B,\psi_{C}+\psi_{A}+\psi_{B})-S_{\rm QCD}(A,\psi_{A})-S_{\rm QCD}(B,\psi_{B})
and

{\tilde{S}}_{C}~=~S_{\rm QCD}({\tilde{C}}+A+B,\tilde{\psi}_{C}+\psi_{A}+\psi_{B})-S_{\rm QCD}(A,\psi_{A})-S_{\rm QCD}(B,\psi_{B})
. It is well known that
in the tree approximation the double functional integral (
11
) is given by a set of
retarded Green functions in the background fields
[
38
,
39
,
40
]
(see also appendix A of ref.
[
29
]
for the proof).
Since the double functional integral (
11
) is given by a set of retarded Green functions
in the background fields
A
and
B
, the calculation of the tree-level contribution to
{\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\psi

in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
11
) is equivalent to solving YM equation for

\psi(x)
and
A_{\mu}(x)
with initial condition that the solution has the same asymptotics at
t\rightarrow-\infty

as the superposition of incoming projectile and target background fields.

The hadronic tensor (
1
) can now be represented as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(p_{A},p_{B},q)~=~{1\over(2\pi)^{4}}\!\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}\langle p_{A}|\langle p_{B}|\hat{\cal O}_{\mu\nu}(q,x;{\hat{A}},{\hat{\psi}}_{A};{\hat{B}},{\hat{\psi}}_{B})|p_{A}\rangle|p_{B}\rangle,

(12)

where
\hat{\cal O}_{\mu\nu}(q,x;{\hat{A}},{\hat{\psi}}_{A};{\hat{B}},{\hat{\psi}}_{B})
should be expanded in a series in
{\hat{A}}
,
{\hat{\psi}}_{A}
,
{\hat{B}}
,
{\hat{\psi}}_{B}
operators
and evaluated between the corresponding (projectile or target) states: if

\hat{\cal O}_{\mu\nu}(q,x;{\hat{A}},{\hat{\psi}}_{A};{\hat{B}},{\hat{\psi}}_{B})~=~\sum_{m,n}\!\int\!dz_{m}dz^{\prime}_{n}c_{m,n}(q,x)\hat{\Phi}_{A}(z_{m})\hat{\Phi}_{B}(z^{\prime}_{n})

(13)

where
c_{m,n}
are coefficients and
\Phi
can be any of
A_{\mu}
,
\psi
or
{\bar{\psi}}
with appropriate Lorentz indices. We get then

\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}~=~\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{4}}\!\int\!d^{4}xe^{-iqx}\sum_{m,n}\!\int\!dz_{m}c_{m,n}(q,x)\langle p_{A}|\hat{\Phi}_{A}(z_{m})|p_{A}\rangle\!\int\!dz^{\prime}_{n}\langle p_{B}|\hat{\Phi}_{B}(z^{\prime}_{n})|p_{B}\rangle.

(14)

As we will demonstrate below, the relevant operators
\hat{\Phi}_{A}
and
\hat{\Phi}_{B}
are quark and gluon fields with Wilson-line type gauge links
collinear to either
p_{2}
for
A
fields or
p_{1}
for
B
fields.

4
Power corrections and solution of classical YM equations

4.1
Power counting for background fields

As we discussed in previous section, to get the hadronic tensor in the form (
12
) we need to calculate
the functional integral (
11
) in the background of the fields (
10
). Since we integrate over fields
(
10
) afterwards, we may assume that they satisfy Yang-Mills equations

4
4

4

As was mentioned above, for the purpose of calculation of integral over
C
fields the projectile and target fields are
“frozen”.

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pti\not{D}_{A}\psi_{A}~=~0,~~~D_{A}^{\nu}A_{\mu\nu}^{a}~=~g^{2}\sum_{f}\bar{\psi}^{f}_{A}\gamma_{\mu}t^{a}\psi^{f}_{A},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pti\not{D}_{B}\psi_{B}~=~0,~~~D_{B}^{\nu}B_{\mu\nu}^{a}~=~g^{2}\sum_{f}\bar{\psi}^{f}_{B}\gamma_{\mu}t^{a}\psi^{f}_{B},

(15)

where
A_{\mu\nu}\equiv\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}-i[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]
,

D_{A}^{\mu}\equiv(\partial^{\mu}-i[A^{\mu},)
and similarly for
B
fields.

5
5

5

Since we are dealing with tree approximation and quark equations of motion, it is convenient to include coupling constant
g

in the definition of gluon fields.

It is convenient to choose a gauge where
A_{\ast}=0
for projectile fields and
B_{\bullet}=0
for target fields.
(The existence of such gauge was proved in appendix B of Ref.
[
29
]
by explicit construction.)
The relative
strength of Lorentz components of projectile and target fields in this gauge was found in ref.
[
29
]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\not{p}_{1}\psi_{A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~\sim~m_{\perp}^{5/2},~~~\gamma_{i}\psi_{A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~\sim~m_{\perp}^{3/2},~~~~~\not{p}_{2}\psi_{A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~\sim~s\sqrt{m_{\perp}},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\not{p}_{1}\psi_{B}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})~\sim~s\sqrt{m_{\perp}},~~~\gamma_{i}\psi_{B}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})~\sim~m_{\perp}^{3/2},~~~~~\not{p}_{2}\psi_{B}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})~\sim~m_{\perp}^{5/2},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA_{\bullet}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~\sim~B_{\ast}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})~\sim~m_{\perp}^{2},~~~~~~A_{i}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~\sim~B_{i}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})~\sim m_{\perp}.

(16)

Here
m_{\perp}
is a scale of order of
m
or
q_{\perp}
.
As discussed in Refs.
[
29
,
28
]
, our rapidity factorization (
14
) is applicable
in the region where
s,Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2},m^{2}
,
while the relation between
q_{\perp}^{2}
and
m^{2}
and between
Q^{2}
and
s
may be arbitrary.
Correspondingly, for the purpose of counting of powers of
s
, we do not distinguish between
s
and
Q^{2}
so our
power counting will be correct at any Bjorken
x
.
The distinction will come at a later time when we specify to small
x
and
disregard
{1\over s}
in comparison to
{1\over Q^{2}}
in final expressions for TMDs and/or coefficient functions. Similarly, for the
purpose of power counting we will not distinguish between
m
and
q_{\perp}
so we
introduce
m_{\perp}
which may be of order of
m
or
q_{\perp}
depending on matrix element.

Note also that in our gauge

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636ptA_{i}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~=~{2\over s}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}~A_{\ast i}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp}),~~~~B_{i}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})~=~{2\over s}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\ast}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\ast}~B_{{\bullet}i}(x^{\prime}_{\ast},x_{\perp})

(17)

where
A_{\ast i}\equiv F^{(A)}_{\ast i}
and
B_{{\bullet}i}\equiv F^{(B)}_{{\bullet}i}
are field strengths for
A
and
B
fields respectively.

Thus, to find TMD factorization formula with power corrections at the tree level we need to calculate the functional integral (
7
) in the background fields of the strength given by eqs. (
16
).

4.2
Approximate solution of classical equations at
q_{\perp}^{2}\ll Q^{2}
.

As we discussed in section
3
, the calculation of the functional integral (
11
) over
C
-fields
in the tree approximation reduces to finding fields
C_{\mu}
and
\psi_{C}
as solutions of Yang-Mills equations for the action

S_{C}~=~S_{\rm QCD}(C+A+B,\psi_{C}+\psi_{A}+\psi_{B})-S_{\rm QCD}(A,\psi_{A})-S_{\rm QCD}(B,\psi_{B})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt(i\not{\partial}+g\not{A}+g\not{B}+g\not{C})(\psi^{f}_{A}+\psi^{f}_{B}+\psi^{f}_{C})~=~0,

(18)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptD^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}(A+B+C)~=~g^{2}\sum_{f}({\bar{\psi}}^{f}_{A}+{\bar{\psi}}^{f}_{B}+{\bar{\psi}}^{f}_{C})\gamma_{\mu}t^{a}(\psi^{f}_{A}+\psi^{f}_{B}+\psi^{f}_{C}).

The solution of eq. (
18
) which we need corresponds to the sum of set of diagrams
in background field
A+B
with
retarded
Green functions, see figure
2
.

Figure 2:
Typical diagram for the classical field with projectile/target sources. The Green functions of central fields are given by retarded propagators.

The sum of tree diagrams with retarded Green functions gives fields
C_{\mu}
and
\psi_{C}
that vanish at
t\rightarrow-\infty
. Thus, we are solving the usual classical YM equations

6
6

6

We take into account only
u,d,s,c
quarks and consider them massless. In principle, one can include “massless”
b
-quark for
q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m_{b}^{2}
.

\mathbb{D}^{\nu}\mathbb{F}^{a}_{\mu\nu}~=~g^{2}\sum_{f}\bar{\Psi}^{f}t^{a}\gamma_{\mu}\Psi^{f},~~~~\not{\mathbb{P}}\Psi^{f}~=~0,

(19)

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}_{\mu}=C_{\mu}+A_{\mu}+B_{\mu},\ \ \ \Psi^{f}=\psi^{f}_{C}+\psi^{f}_{A}+\psi^{f}_{B},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mu}~\equiv~i\partial_{\mu}+C_{\mu}+A_{\mu}+B_{\mu},~~~~\mathbb{F}_{\mu\nu}~=~\partial_{\mu}\mathbb{A}_{\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu-i[\mathbb{A}_{\mu},\mathbb{A}_{\nu}],

(20)

with boundary conditions

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt\mathbb{A}_{\mu}(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x_{\ast}\rightarrow-\infty}}{{=}}A_{\mu}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}),~~~~\Psi(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x_{\ast}\rightarrow-\infty}}{{=}}\psi_{A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt\mathbb{A}_{\mu}(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x_{\bullet}\rightarrow-\infty}}{{=}}B_{\mu}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp}),~~~~\Psi(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x_{\bullet}\rightarrow-\infty}}{{=}}\psi_{B}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})

(21)

following from
C_{\mu},\psi_{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t\rightarrow-\infty}}{{\rightarrow}}0
.
These boundary conditions reflect the fact that at
t\rightarrow-\infty
we have only incoming hadrons with
A
and
B
fields.

As discussed in Ref.
[
29
]
, for our case of particle production with
{q_{\perp}\over Q}\ll 1
it is possible to find
the approximate solution of (
19
) as a series in this small parameter.
One solves Eqs. (
19
) iteratively, order by order in perturbation theory, starting from the
zero-order approximation in the form of the sum of projectile and target fields

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\mathbb{A}_{\mu}^{[0]}(x)~=~A_{\mu}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})+B_{\mu}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\Psi^{[0]}(x)~=~\psi_{A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})+\psi_{B}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})

(22)

and improving it by calculation of Feynman diagrams with retarded propagators in the background fields (
22
).

Let me now explain how the parameter
{m_{\perp}^{2}/s}
comes up in the rapidity-factorization approach
(for details, see Ref.
[
29
]
).
When we expand quark and gluon propagators in powers of background fields,
we get a set of diagrams shown in figure
2
.
The typical bare gluon propagator in figure
2
is

{1\over p^{2}+i\epsilon p_{0}}~=~{1\over\alpha\beta s-p_{\perp}^{2}+i\epsilon(\alpha+\beta)}.

(23)

In the tree approximation, the transverse momenta
in tree diagrams are determined by further integration over projectile (“A”) and target (“B”) fields
in eq. (
7
) which converge on either
q_{\perp}
or
m_{N}
. On the other hand, the integrals over

\alpha
converge on either
\alpha_{q}
or
\alpha\sim 1
and similarly the characteristic
\beta
’s
are either
\beta_{q}
or
\beta\sim 1
.
Since
\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s=Q_{\parallel}^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
, one can expand gluon and quark propagators
in powers of
{p_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha\beta s}

\displaystyle{1\over p^{2}+i\epsilon p_{0}}~=~{1\over s(\alpha+i\epsilon)(\beta+i\epsilon)}\Big(1+{p_{\perp}^{2}/s\over(\alpha+i\epsilon)(\beta+i\epsilon)}+...\Big),

(24)

\displaystyle{\not{p}\over p^{2}+i\epsilon p_{0}}~=~{1\over s}\Big({\not{p}_{1}\over\beta+i\epsilon}+{\not{p}_{2}\over\alpha+i\epsilon}+{\not{p}_{\perp}\over(\alpha+i\epsilon)(\beta+i\epsilon)}\Big)\Big(1+{p_{\perp}^{2}/s\over(\alpha+i\epsilon)(\beta+i\epsilon)}+...\Big).

After the expansion (
24
), the dynamics in the transverse space effectively becomes trivial:
all background fields stand either at
x
or at
0
. Note that in this statement is solely a consequence of

Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
and does not rely on small-
x
approximation.

4.3
Power expansion of classical quark fields

Now we expand the classical quark fields in powers of
{p_{\perp}^{2}\over p_{\parallel}^{2}}\sim{m_{\perp}^{2}\over s}

(the corresponding expansion of classical gluon fields is presented in Ref.
[
29
]
, but we do not need it here).
As demonstrated in Ref.
[
28
]
,
expanding it in powers of
p^{2}_{\perp}/p_{\parallel}^{2}
we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\Psi(x)~=~\Psi_{1}(x)+\Psi_{2}(x)+\dots,

(25)

where

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\Psi_{1}~=~\psi_{A}+\Xi_{1},~~~~\Xi_{1}~=~-{\not{p}_{2}\over s}\gamma^{i}B_{i}{1\over\alpha+i\epsilon}\psi_{A}~=~{i\over s}\sigma_{\ast i}B^{i}{1\over\alpha+i\epsilon}\psi_{A},

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}~=~\bar{\psi}_{A}+{\bar{\Xi}}_{1},~~~~{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}~=~-\big(\bar{\psi}_{A}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\big)\gamma^{i}B_{i}{\not{p}_{2}\over s}~=~-{i\over s}\big(\bar{\psi}_{A}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\big)B^{i}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\Psi_{2}~=~\psi_{B}+\Xi_{2},~~~~\Xi_{2}~=~-{\not{p}_{1}\over s}\gamma^{i}A_{i}{1\over\beta+i\epsilon}\psi_{B}~=~{i\over s}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}A^{i}{1\over\beta+i\epsilon}\psi_{B},

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt{\bar{\Psi}}_{2}~=~\bar{\psi}_{B}+{\bar{\Xi}}_{2},~~~~{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}~=~-\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta-i\epsilon}\big)\gamma^{i}A_{i}{\not{p}_{1}\over s}~=~-{i\over s}\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta-i\epsilon}\big)A_{i}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}

(26)

and dots stand for terms subleading in
{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
and/or
\alpha_{q},\beta_{q}
parameters (hereafter we assume the small-
x
approximation

\alpha_{q},\beta_{q}\ll 1
in all calculations).
In this formula

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over\alpha+i\epsilon}\psi_{A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~\equiv~-i\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}~\psi_{A}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\Big({\bar{\psi}}_{A}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\Big)(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~\equiv~i\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}~{\bar{\psi}}_{A}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp})

(27)

and similarly for
{1\over\beta\pm i\epsilon}
.
For brevity, in what follows we denote
\big(\bar{\psi}_{A}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\equiv\big({\bar{\psi}}_{A}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\big)(x)
and

\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}{1\over\beta}\big)(x)\equiv\big({\bar{\psi}}_{B}{1\over\beta-i\epsilon}\big)(x)
.
Let us estimate the relative size of corrections
\Xi
in Eq. (
26
) at small
x
. As we will see,
{1\over\alpha}
and
{1\over\beta}
transform
to
{1\over\alpha_{q}}
and
{1\over\beta_{q}}
in our TMDs so

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\Xi_{1}~\sim~\psi_{A}{m_{\perp}\over\alpha_{q}\sqrt{s}}~\sim~\psi_{A}{q_{\perp}\over Q},~~~~\Xi_{2}~\sim~\psi_{B}{m_{\perp}\over\beta_{q}\sqrt{s}}~\sim~\psi_{B}{q_{\perp}\over Q}

(28)

if
\alpha_{q}\sim\beta_{q}\sim{Q\over\sqrt{s}}
(recall that we assume that the DY pair is emitted in the central region of rapidity).
For example, the correction
\sim[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}]
will be of order of
{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
in comparison to leading-twist contribution
[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}]
.

7
7

7

The reader may wonder why there are no corrections
\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
coming from next terms in the expansion (
25
)
like
[{\bar{\psi}}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)][{\bar{\psi}}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}{\gamma^{i}\over s}\not{p}_{2}{1\over\beta}{1\over\alpha}\gamma^{j}\partial_{i}B_{j}\Psi_{A}(0)]
. The reason is that

{1\over\beta}
between
{\bar{\psi}}_{B}(0)
and
B_{j}(0)
does
not
transform to
{1\over\beta_{q}}
and remains
\sim O(1)
, see the discussion
in the Appendix 8.3.4 of Ref.
[
28
]
.

5
Hadronic tensor at
s\gg Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}

In general, our method is applicable for calculation of power corrections
at any
s,Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2},m^{2}_{N}
. However, the expressions are greatly simplified
in the physically interesting case
s\gg Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
which is considered in this paper.

As we noted above, we take into account only hadronic tensor due to electromagnetic currents of
u,d,s,c
quarks and consider these quarks to be massless.
It is convenient to define coordinate-space hadronic tensor multiplied by
N_{c}{2\over s}
(and denoted by extra “check” mark) as follows

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}(p_{A},p_{B},x)~\equiv~N_{c}{2\over s}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|J_{\mu}(x)J_{\nu}(0)|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle

(29)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(p_{A},p_{B},q)~=~{s/2\over(2\pi)^{4}N_{c}}\int\!d^{4}x~e^{-iqx}\check{W}_{\mu\nu}(p_{A},p_{B},x).

For future use, let us also define the hadronic tensor in mixed representation: in momentum longitudinal
space but in transverse coordinate space

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(p_{A},p_{B},q)~=~\int\!d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{i(q,x)_{\perp}}W_{\mu\nu}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},x_{\perp}),

(30)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},x_{\perp})~\equiv~{1\over(2\pi)^{4}}{2\over s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\ast}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}-i\beta_{q}x_{\ast}}\langle p_{A},p_{B}|J_{\mu}(x_{\bullet},x_{\ast},x_{\perp})J_{\nu}(0)|p_{A},p_{B}\rangle.

After integration over central fields in the tree approximation we obtain

\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}(p_{A},p_{B},x)~\equiv~N_{c}{2\over s}\langle A,B|J_{\mu}(x_{\bullet},x_{\ast},x_{\perp})J_{\nu}(0)|A,B\rangle

(31)

where

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptJ^{\mu}~=~J^{\mu}_{A}+J^{\mu}_{B}+J^{\mu}_{AB}+J^{\mu}_{BA},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptJ^{\mu}_{A}~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}\bar{\Psi}_{1}^{f}\gamma^{\mu}\Psi_{1}^{f},~~~J^{\mu}_{AB}~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}\bar{\Psi}_{1}^{f}\gamma^{\mu}\Psi_{2}^{f}

(32)

and similarly for
J^{\mu}_{B}
and
J^{\mu}_{BA}
. Here
\langle A,B|{\cal O}(\psi_{A},A_{\mu},\psi_{B},B_{\mu})|A,B\rangle
denotes
double functional integral over
A
and
B
fields which gives matrix elements between projectile and target states of Eq. (
14
) type.

The leading-twist contribution to
W_{\mu\nu}(q)
comes only from product
J_{AB}^{\mu}(x)J_{BA}^{\nu}(0)
(or
J_{BA}^{\mu}(x)J_{AB}^{\nu}(0)
),
while power corrections may come also from other terms like
J_{A}^{\mu}(x)J_{B}^{\nu}(0)
. We will consider all terms in turn.

5.1
Leading-twist contribution and
{1\over Q^{2}}
terms from
J_{AB}^{\mu}(x)J_{BA}^{\nu}(0)

Power expansion of
J_{AB}^{\mu}(x)J_{BA}^{\nu}(0)
reads

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma^{\mu}\Psi_{2}(x){\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma^{\nu}\Psi_{1}(0)~+~...

(33)

where quark fields are given by Eq. (
26
).
As we mentioned above, in Ref.
[
28
]
it is demonstrated that terms neglected in the r.h.s. lead to power corrections
\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}s}
or

\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over\beta_{q}s}
which are much smaller than
{q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}={q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}_{\|}}\sim{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
(if DY pair
is emitted in the central region of rapidity). Note that since we want to calculate the leading power corrections, we can substitute
Q^{2}_{\parallel}
with
Q^{2}
.
In the limit
s\gg Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
this change of variables can only lead to errors of the order of subleading power terms.

8
8

8

Except for the leading-twist term where the difference between
Q^{2}_{\parallel}
and
Q^{2}
matters.

As to terms
\sim{\bar{\Psi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Psi_{2}(x){\bar{\Psi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Psi_{1}(0)
, they can be decomposed
using eq. (
26
) as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\big[\big(\bar{\psi}_{A}+{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}\big)(x)\gamma_{\mu}\big(\psi_{B}+\Xi_{2}\big)(x)\big][\big(\bar{\psi}_{B}+{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}\big)(0)\gamma_{\nu}\big(\psi_{A}+\Xi_{1}\big)(0)\big]~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]

(34)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]+[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]~+~x\leftrightarrow 0.

where the square brackets mean trace over Lorentz and color indices.

First, let us consider the leading-twist term coming from the first term in the r.h.s. of this equation.

5.2
Leading-twist contribution

As we mentioned, the leading-twist term comes from
J_{AB}^{\mu}(x)J_{BA}^{\nu}(0)
and

J_{BA}^{\mu}(x)J_{AB}^{\nu}(0)
. Using Fierz transformation (
237
) one obtains

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{N_{c}\over s}\big(\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big)~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s}\big[-({\bar{\psi}}_{A}\psi_{A})({\bar{\psi}}_{B}\psi_{B})+({\bar{\psi}}_{A}\gamma_{5}\psi_{A})({\bar{\psi}}_{B}\gamma_{5}\psi_{B})+(\psi_{A}\gamma_{\alpha}\psi_{A})({\bar{\psi}}_{B}\gamma^{\alpha}\psi_{B})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~(\psi_{A}\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\psi_{A})({\bar{\psi}}_{B}\gamma^{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\psi_{B})-{1\over 2}(\psi_{A}\sigma^{\alpha\beta}\psi_{A})({\bar{\psi}}_{B}\sigma_{\alpha\beta}\psi_{B})\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{1\over 2s}[(\psi_{A}\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{A})({\bar{\psi}}_{B}\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{B})+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]-{1\over 2s}(\psi_{A}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\psi_{A})({\bar{\psi}}_{B}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\psi_{B})+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over 2s}[(\psi_{A}\sigma_{\nu\alpha}\psi_{A})({\bar{\psi}}_{B}\sigma_{\mu\alpha}\psi_{B})+(\psi_{A}\sigma_{\mu\alpha}\psi_{A})({\bar{\psi}}_{B}\sigma_{\nu\alpha}\psi_{B})]~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(35)

where all parentheses in the r.h.s. are color singlet.
As usual, after integration over background fields
A
and
B
we promote
A
,
\psi_{A}
and
B
,
\psi_{B}
to operators
{\hat{A}}
,
\hat{\psi}
.
A subtle point is that our operators are not under T-product ordering so one should be careful while changing the order of operators in
formulas like Fierz transformation. Fortunately, all operators in the r.h.s of Eq. (
35
) are separated either by space-like intervals or light-like intervals so they commute with each other.

From parametrization of two-quark operators in section
11.2
,
it is clear that the leading-twist contribution to
W_{\mu\nu}(q)
comes from

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{\rm lt}_{\mu\nu}~=~{1\over 2s}(g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}-\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}-\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta})\langle\hat{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\gamma_{\alpha}{\hat{\psi}}(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\hat{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\beta}{\hat{\psi}}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})\rangle_{B}

(36)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over 2s}\big(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-{1\over 2}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\big)\langle\hat{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\sigma_{\alpha\xi}{\hat{\psi}}(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\hat{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\beta}^{~\xi}{\hat{\psi}}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})\rangle_{B}~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

Hereafter we use notations
\langle{\cal O}\rangle_{A}\equiv\langle p_{A}|{\cal O}|p_{A}\rangle
and

\langle{\cal O}\rangle_{B}\equiv\langle p_{B}|{\cal O}|p_{B}\rangle
for brevity
9
9

9

In a general gauge for
projectile and target fields these matrix elements read

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\langle p_{A}|{\hat{\psi}}_{f}(x)\gamma_{\mu}{\hat{\psi}}_{f}(0)|p_{A}\rangle~=~\langle p_{A}|{\hat{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\gamma_{\mu}[x_{\bullet},-\infty_{\bullet}]_{x}[x_{\perp},0_{\perp}]_{-\infty_{\bullet}}[-\infty_{\bullet},0_{\bullet}]_{0}{\hat{\psi}}_{f}(0)|p_{A}\rangle,

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\langle p_{B}|{\hat{\psi}}_{f}(x)\gamma_{\mu}{\hat{\psi}}_{f}(0)|p_{B}\rangle~=~\langle p_{B}|{\hat{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})\gamma_{\mu}[x_{\ast},-\infty_{\ast}]_{x}[x_{\perp},0_{\perp}]_{-\infty_{\ast}}[-\infty_{\ast},0_{\ast}]_{0}{\hat{\psi}}_{f}(0)|p_{B}\rangle

(37)

and similarly for other operators.

. The corresponding leading-twist contribution to to
W_{\mu\nu}(q)

has the form
[
41
]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}^{\rm lt}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},q_{\perp})~=~{1\over 16\pi^{4}N_{c}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\ast}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}-i\beta_{q}x_{\ast}+i(q,x)_{\perp}}\check{W}^{\rm lt}_{\mu\nu}(x)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}{1\over N_{c}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big(-g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}\big[f_{1}^{f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}){\bar{f}}_{1}^{f}(\beta_{q},q_{\perp}-k_{\perp})+{\bar{f}}_{1}^{f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})f_{1}^{f}(\beta_{q},q_{\perp}-k_{\perp})\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36967pt-~\big[k^{\perp}_{\mu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}+k^{\perp}_{\nu}(q-k)^{\perp}_{\mu}+g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt\times~\big[h^{\perp}_{1f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}){\bar{h}}^{\perp}_{1f}(\beta_{q},q_{\perp}-k_{\perp})+{\bar{h}}^{\perp}_{1f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})h^{\perp}_{1f}(\beta_{q},q_{\perp}-k_{\perp})\big]\Big)

(38)

Let us discuss other terms proportional to different TMDs in parametrizations in Sect.
11.2
. To this end, we write down terms from Eq. (
6
) that we are looking for in Sudakov variables:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptg_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}\Big[1+{q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}\Big],~~{q_{\perp}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big[1+{q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}\Big],~~g_{\parallel}^{\mu\nu}\Big[0+{q_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}\beta_{q}s}\Big],~~{2\over\alpha_{q}s}\big(p_{2}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big),~~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{2\over\beta_{q}s}\big(p_{1}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\big),~~{p_{1}^{\mu}p_{1}^{\nu}\over\beta_{q}^{2}s^{2}},~~{p_{2}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}\over\alpha_{q}^{2}s^{2}}

(39)

Here zero in the third term means that the contribution of order one is actually absent.
As discussed in Sect.
11.2
, all TMDs considered here can have only logarithmic dependence on Bjorken
x
(
\equiv\alpha_{q}
or
\beta_{q}
)
but
not
the power dependence
{1\over x}
. It is easy to see that other quark-antiquark TMDs give contributions to
W_{\mu}(q)
which look like terms in Eq. (
39
) but without extra
{1\over\alpha_{q}}
and/or
{1\over\beta_{q}}
so they are power suppressed in low-x regime

s\gg Q^{2}
.

Let us also specify the terms which we do not calculate. Roughly speaking, they correspond to terms in Eq. (
39
) multiplied by

{m_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
or by either
\alpha_{q}
or
\beta_{q}
. Our strategy in the next sections is to compare a certain term in

\check{W}_{\mu\nu}
to terms in Eq. (
39
), and, if it is smaller, neglect, if it is of the same size, calculate.

6
Terms coming from
J_{AB}^{\mu}(x)J_{BA}^{\nu}(0)

We separate terms in Eq. (
34
) according to number of gluon fields (contained in
\Xi
’s ).

\check{W}_{\mu\nu}~\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm sym}~\mu,\nu}}{{=}}~\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{\rm lt}+\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(1)}+\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(2a)}+\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(2b)}+\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(2c)}

(40)

where leading-twist terms without gluons (quark-antiquark TMDs) were considered in previous Section, and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(1)}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(41)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(2a)}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(42)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(2b)}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(43)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(2c)}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{2}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(44)

The corresponding contributions to
W_{\mu\nu}(q)
will be denoted
W_{\mu\nu}^{(1)}
,
W_{\mu\nu}^{(2)a}
,
W_{\mu\nu}^{(2)b}
, and

W_{\mu\nu}^{(2)c}
, respectively.
We will consider these contributions in turn.

6.1
Terms with one quark-quark-gluon operator

In this section we consider terms in Eq. (
41
) which will lead to
{2\over\beta_{q}s}p_{1}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
and

{2\over\alpha_{q}s}p_{2}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
contributions to
W_{\mu\nu}(q)
.

6.1.1
Term with
\Xi_{1}

Let us start with the last term in Eq. (
41
). The Fierz transformation (
237
) yields

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 2}[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{g_{\mu\nu}\over 4}\big\{\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}^{m}(x){{\not\!p}_{2}\over s}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi_{A}^{k}(0)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}^{n}{\bar{B}}_{i}^{nk}(0)\psi_{B}^{m}(x)\big]~-~(\psi_{A}^{k}\otimes\psi_{B}^{n}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi_{A}^{k}\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi_{B}^{n}\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over 4}(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\big\{\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}^{m}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}{{\not\!p}_{2}\over s}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi_{A}^{k}(0)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}^{n}{\bar{B}}_{i}^{nk}(0)\gamma_{\beta}\psi^{m}_{B}(x)\big]~+~(\gamma_{\alpha}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\leftrightarrow\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5})\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{1\over 4}(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-{1\over 2}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta})\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}^{m}(x)\sigma_{\alpha\xi}{{\not\!p}_{2}\over s}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi_{A}^{k}(0)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}^{n}{\bar{B}}_{i}^{nk}(0)\sigma_{\beta}^{~\xi}\psi^{m}_{B}(x)\big]

(45)

where we used Eq. (
26
)
\Xi_{1}(0)=-{{\not\!p}_{2}\over s}\gamma^{i}{\bar{B}}_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi_{A}(0)
. Note that all
colors are in the fundamental representation so e.g.
B^{mn}(x)\equiv(t_{a})^{mn}B^{a}(x)
.

Promoting
A
and
B
fields to operators and sorting out the color-singlet contributions we get

10
10

10

We will keep different notations
A_{i}
and
B_{i}
for the projectile and target gluon fields because of the relations
(
246
) and (
250
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{1\mu\nu}^{(1)}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|[\hat{\bar{\psi}}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}{\hat{\psi}}_{B}(x)\big]\big[\hat{\bar{\psi}}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{2}}\big\{\langle\hat{\bar{\psi}}(x){\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}{\hat{\psi}}(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\hat{\bar{\psi}}{\bar{B}}_{i}(0){\hat{\psi}}(x)\rangle_{B}~-~({\hat{\psi}}(0)\otimes{\hat{\psi}}(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}{\hat{\psi}}(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}{\hat{\psi}}(x)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over 2s^{2}}(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta})\big\{\langle\hat{\bar{\psi}}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}{\hat{\psi}}(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\hat{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\gamma_{\beta}{\hat{\psi}}(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt~+~({\hat{\psi}}(0)\otimes{\hat{\psi}}(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}{\hat{\psi}}(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}{\hat{\psi}}(x)\big\}

(46)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{1\over 2s^{2}}(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-{1\over 2}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta})\langle\hat{\bar{\psi}}(x)\sigma_{\alpha\xi}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}{\hat{\psi}}(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\hat{\bar{\psi}}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\beta}^{~\xi}{\hat{\psi}}(x)\rangle_{B}~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

It is convenient to treat terms
\sim g_{\mu\nu}
separately so we define

\check{W}_{1\mu\nu}^{(1)}(x)~=~\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(1a)}(x)+\check{W}_{1\mu\nu}^{(1b)}(x)
where

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(1a)}(x)~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{2}}\Big(\big\{\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}{\bar{B}}_{i}(0)\psi(x)]\rangle_{B}~-~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-~\big\{\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma_{\alpha}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\gamma^{\alpha}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~{1\over 2}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\alpha\beta}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma^{\alpha\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(47)

Hereafter we omit “hat” notation from from operators:
\langle{\cal O}\rangle_{A,B}\equiv\langle\hat{{\cal O}}\rangle_{A,B}
for brevity.

Let us now estimate this contribution to
\check{W}_{\mu\nu}
. First, recall that
B_{i}
is of order of
m_{\perp}
(more accurately, it will be
\sim q_{i}
after the Fourier transformation, see e.g. Eq. (
278
) or Eq. (
284
)). Next, as demonstrated in Sect.
11.3
(see Eqs. (
266
), (
267
)),

{1\over\alpha}
in the target matrix element turns to
\pm{1\over\alpha_{q}}
after Fourier transformation. Due to this fact we will replace
{1\over\alpha}
by
{1\over\alpha_{q}}
in our estimates, even in the coordinate space. Similarly, for the estimate of the target matrix elements we will replaces operator
{1\over\beta}
by
{1\over\beta_{q}}
whenever appropriate.

Now we will demonstrate that three terms in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
47
) are small in comparison to
terms listed in Eq. (
39
). The projectile matrix element in the first term in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
47
) brings factor
s
(see Eq. (
265
)) but the target matrix element
can produce only factor
x_{i}
so the first term is
\sim{g_{\mu\nu}m_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}s}
which is smaller than

{g_{\mu\nu}q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
that we have in Eq. (
39
)
(and will calculate in the next Section). As to the second term in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
46
),
it can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\big\{\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\gamma^{j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+{2\over s}\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0){\not\!p}_{2}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{2}}

(48)

The projectile matrix element in the first term in the r.h.s. of this equation brings factor
s
but, as we discussed above, the target matrix element
cannot produce factor
s
so this term is again
\sim{g_{\mu\nu}m_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}s}\ll{g_{\mu\nu}m_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
.
As to the second term,
converting three
\gamma
-matrices in the projectile matrix element to a combination of
\gamma
’s and
\gamma\gamma_{5}
’s and looking
at the parametrization of Sect.
11.2
, we see that
{2\over s}\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

is not proportional to
s
. In addition, as discuss in Sect.
11.2
, the target matrix element

\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\gamma_{\mu}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

knows about
p_{1}
only via the direction of Wilson lines so it can be proportional only to
{p_{1\mu}\over p_{1}\cdot p_{2}}
that does not change at
rescaling of
p_{1}
.
Thus,
\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0){\not\!p}_{2}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}
is
\sim O(1)

and therefore the second term in Eq. (
48
) is even smaller than the first one.
Finally, let us discuss the third term in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
47
). If both
\alpha
and
\beta
are transverse

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{g_{\mu\nu}\over 4s^{2}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\alpha\beta}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma^{\alpha\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\sim{g_{\mu\nu}m_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}s}

(49)

similarly to the first term in Eq. (
48
). If both indices are longitudinal, we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{g_{\mu\nu}\over s^{4}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\ast{\bullet}}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\ast}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt=~{g_{\mu\nu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\ast}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(50)

The projectile matrix element brings a factor
s
, but the target one is
\sim O(1)
due to the reason discussed above, so this
contribution is negligible. Finally, let us consider the case when index
\alpha
is longitudinal and
\beta
is transverse

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\ast}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(51)

Again, the target matrix element is
\sim O(1)
while the projectile one
can bring one factor of
s
as can be seen from parametrization (
265
) by reducing the number of
\gamma
-matrices to two.
Thus, the contribution (
51
) is negligible and so is the total contribution (
47
).

We get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(1)}(x)~\simeq~\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(1b)}(x)~=~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{1\over 2s^{2}}\big\{\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma_{\mu}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{1\over 2s^{2}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\mu\xi}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(52)

Let us start with the case when both of the indices
\mu
and
\nu
are transverse. It is easy to see that the power counting for the
first term in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
52
) is the same as for Eq. (
48
) so it is small. Also, the estimate of the second term
in Eq. (
52
) is similar either to the estimate of Eq. (
49
) or (
51
) so it can be neglected.

Next, let us consider both
\mu
and
\nu
longitudinal. It is easy to see that multiplication of the r.h.s. of Eq. (
52
) by

p_{2}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}
gives zero so there is no term proportional to
p_{1}^{\mu}p_{1}^{\nu}
. The term proportional to
p_{2}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}

has the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{4\over s^{4}}p_{2\mu}p_{2\nu}\big\{\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{4\over s^{4}}p_{2\mu}p_{2\nu}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\xi}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~\xi}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(53)

It is easy to see that both projectile and target matrix elements are proportional to the first power of
s
so the resulting
estimate is
{p_{2\mu}p_{2\nu}\over\alpha_{q}s^{2}}m_{\perp}^{2}
which is
\sim O\big({m_{\perp}^{2}\over s}\big)
in comparison to
the corrseponding term in Eq. (
39
). If one index is
p_{1}
and the other
p_{2}
we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{g^{\parallel}_{\mu\nu}\over s^{3}}\Big[\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0){\not\!p}_{2}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)

(54)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\ast}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-~{4\over s}\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\ast}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\Big]

It is easy to see that in all terms the projectile matrix element is
\sim s
but the target one is
\sim O(1)
so
the corresponding contribution
\sim{g_{\mu\nu}m_{\perp}^{2}\over s^{2}}
is negligible.

Finally, let us consider the case when one of the indices in Eq. (
52
) is longitudinal and one transverse. For example,
let
\mu
be longitudinal and
\nu
transverse, the opposite case
will differ by replacement
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
.
Using the decomposition of
g^{\mu\nu}
in longitudinal and transverse part (
8
)
we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\Big({2p_{1\mu}p_{2}^{\mu^{\prime}}\over s}+p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}\Big)\check{W}_{\mu\nu}^{(1b)}(x)~=~\Big({p_{2\mu}p_{1}^{\mu^{\prime}}\over s^{3}}+p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\Big[\big\{\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma_{\mu^{\prime}}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\mu^{\prime}\xi}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~\mu^{\prime}\leftrightarrow\nu\Big]~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

(55)

The term proportional to
p_{2\mu}
in the r.h.s. can be expressed using Eq. (
249
) as follows

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\Big\{\Big[\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\Big]

(56)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt-~\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\xi}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~\xi}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}\xi}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~\xi}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\Big\{\Big[-\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{\nu_{\perp}}(0)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt+{s\over 2}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma^{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\breve{B}_{i}(0)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x))\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt+~i\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+i\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}j}\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt-~\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+{2i\over s}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\ast{\bullet}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\Big\}

Hereafter we use notation
\breve{B}_{i}\equiv B_{i}-i{\tilde{B}}_{i}\gamma_{5}
.

Let us at evaluate two the most important contributions. The first is

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{\nu_{\perp}}(0)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

(57)

As we shall see below, due to QCD equations of motion
{\not\!\!B}
in the r.h.s. of this equation can be replaced by transverse momentum of the target TMD
k_{\perp}
. Also,
{1\over\alpha}
will be replaced by

so from the parametrizations (

) and (

) we see that

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~\sim~{p_{2\mu}\over\alpha_{q}s}k_{\nu}f{\bar{f}}

which is of order of fourth term in Eq. (

).
The second relevant term is

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{ip_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}j}\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\ast}^{~j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)[B_{\nu}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}-\nu\leftrightarrow j]\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~-~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\ast\nu_{\perp}}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt\times~\langle\bar{\psi}B^{j}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~=~{ip_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\ast\nu_{\perp}}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

where we used formula (
240
)
and the fact that for unpolarized protons

\langle p|\bar{\psi}(0)[A_{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}-i\leftrightarrow j]\psi(x)|p\rangle=0

(60)

from parity conservation.

11

A rigorous argument goes like that: the matrix element (

) can be rewritten as

\epsilon_{\nu_{\perp}j}\epsilon_{kl}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)[A_{k}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}l}\psi(x)\rangle~=~\epsilon_{j\nu_{\perp}}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!\!A}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\rangle
.
As demonstrated in Sect.

,
{\not\!\!A}
in this formula can be replaced by
{\not\!k}_{\perp}
so the contribution is
proportional to matrix element

which vanishes as seen from the parametrization (

).

Again,
{1\over\alpha}

will turn to
{1\over\alpha_{q}}
and
{\not\!\!B}
can be replaced by

for the target, so (

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{ip_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\ast\nu_{\perp}}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~\sim~{p_{2\mu}\over\alpha_{q}s}k_{\nu}h{\bar{h}}

Let us demonstrate that the remaining terms in the r.h.s. of Eq. (

First, term coming from replacement
\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)

in Eq. (

\langle\bar{\psi}(x){\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}=0
for unpolarized hadrons,

is small because neither projectile no target matrix elements can bring factor

Last, using Eq. (

) we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{ip_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\big[\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt+~{2\over s}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\ast{\bullet}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{ip_{2\mu}\over 2s^{2}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\big[g_{ij}+i\epsilon_{ij}\gamma_{5}+i(\sigma_{ij}-{2\over s}g_{ij}\sigma_{{\bullet}\ast})\big]\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt+~{i\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\big[g_{i\nu_{\perp}}+i\epsilon_{i\nu_{\perp}}\gamma_{5}+i(\sigma_{i\nu_{\perp}}-{2\over s}g_{i\nu_{\perp}}\sigma_{{\bullet}\ast})\big]\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0)\sigma_{\ast{\bullet}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

It is easy to see that neither the projectile nor the target matrix element
in the r.h.s. of this equation gives
s
so these terms can be neglected in comparison to Eqs. (

)
and (

Thus, the two non-negligible terms in Eq. (

) give

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}^{(1)}_{1\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle+x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\big[\langle\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){\not\!p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}{\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{\perp}_{\nu}\psi(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt+~i\langle\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\sigma_{\ast\nu}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}{\not\!\!B}(0){\hat{p}}_{1}\psi(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})\rangle_{B}\big]~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

Using formulas (

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt\times~\langle A,B|\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]+x\leftrightarrow 0|A,B\rangle~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{1\over 64\pi^{6}N_{c}}{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\!\int d^{2}k_{\perp}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\!\int\!dx_{\ast}d^{2}x^{\prime}_{\perp}e^{-i\beta x_{\ast}+i(q-k,x^{\prime})_{\perp}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times~\big[\langle\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){\not\!p}_{2}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}{\not\!\!B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{\perp}_{\nu}\psi(x_{\ast},x^{\prime}_{\perp})\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~i\langle\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\sigma_{\ast\nu}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}{\not\!\!B}(0){\hat{p}}_{1}\psi(x_{\ast},x^{\prime}_{\perp})\rangle_{B}+x\leftrightarrow 0\big]~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over\alpha_{q}sN_{c}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{(q-k)_{\nu}\big[f_{1}^{f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}){\bar{f}}_{\perp}^{f}(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp})+{\bar{f}}_{1}^{f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})f_{\perp}^{f}(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp})\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~k_{\nu}{(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}\big[h_{1f}^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}){\bar{h}}_{\perp}^{f}(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp})+{\bar{h}}_{1f}^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})h_{\perp}^{f}(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp})\big]\Big\}~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

where terms with replacement

and

come from
x\leftrightarrow 0
contribution.

Next we consider the remaining
\sim p_{1\mu}
term in Eq. (

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{2p_{1\mu}\over s^{2}}N_{c}\langle A,B|[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x){\not\!p}_{2}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]|A,B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{1\mu}\over s^{3}}\big\{\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0){\not\!p}_{2}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{p_{1\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B_{i}(0)\sigma_{\ast\nu_{\perp}}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~{ip_{1\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}j}\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B_{i}(0)\sigma_{\ast}^{~j}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{1\mu}\over s^{3}}\big\{\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B^{i}(0){\not\!p}_{2}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)]\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{p_{1\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\ast i}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)[B_{i}(0)\sigma_{\ast\nu_{\perp}}+i\leftrightarrow\nu_{\perp}]\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~{p_{1\mu}\over s^{3}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\sigma_{\ast\nu_{\perp}}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}B_{i}(0)\sigma_{\ast}^{~i}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

only through direction of Wilson lines so they should not change under rescaling

\sim{p_{1\mu}\over\alpha_{q}s^{2}}k^{\perp}_{\nu}

so the corresponding contribution to

in comparison to that of Eq. (

Thus, the contribution of the first term in Eq. (
41
) to

is

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{(1)}_{1\mu\nu}(q)~=~{1\over 16\pi^{4}}{1\over s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\ast}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}-i\beta x_{\ast}+i(q,x)_{\perp}}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt\times~\langle A,B|\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(0)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(0)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(x)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(x)\big]+(x\leftrightarrow 0)|A,B\rangle+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over\alpha_{q}N_{c}}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big[(q-k)^{\perp}_{\nu}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})-~k_{\nu}{(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})\Big]~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

) with

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802ptH^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~=~h^{\perp}_{1f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}){\bar{h}}^{\perp}_{1f}(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp})~+~h_{1f}^{\perp}\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}_{1f}^{\perp}

Let us consider now the second term in Eq. (
41
). The calculation repeats that of the first term so we will indicate here main steps and pay attention to non-negligible terms only. If one of the indices (say,
\mu
) is longitudinal and the other transverse, we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\Big({2p_{1\mu}p_{2}^{\mu^{\prime}}\over s}+p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}\Big){N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu^{\prime}}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]~+~\mu^{\prime}\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~\langle A,B|p_{2\mu}\big(\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x){\not\!p}_{1}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]~+\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\psi_{A}(0)\big]\big)

The most important terms are those proportional to

. Using Fierz transformation and separating color singlets, they can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\Big\{\big[\langle{\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}(x)\gamma^{i}{\not\!p}_{2}{\not\!p}_{1}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\nu}^{\perp}B_{i}(x)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\langle{\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}(x)\gamma^{i}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{\perp}_{\nu}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0){\not\!p}_{1}B_{i}(x)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt-~\langle{\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}(x)\gamma^{i}{\not\!p}_{2}\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}\xi}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~\xi}B_{i}(x)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\Big\}~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

After some algebra with
\gamma
-matrices this can be transformed to

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{(1)}_{2\mu\nu}(x)~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over s^{3}}\Big\{\langle{\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}(x){\not\!p}_{2}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\nu}^{\perp}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!\!B}(x)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt-~i\langle{\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}(x)\sigma_{\ast\nu_{\perp}}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle{\bar{\psi}}(0){\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!\!B}(x)\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\Big\}~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

plus terms small in comparison to

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~i\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}~d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Gamma\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{1\over\alpha_{q}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Gamma\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{4}}{1\over s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\ast}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}-i\beta x_{\ast}+i(q,x)_{\perp}}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt\times~\langle A,B|\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{B}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{2\mu}\over\alpha_{q}sN_{c}}\Big[(q-k)_{\nu}f_{1}^{f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}){\bar{f}}_{\perp}^{f}(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp})

\Xi_{2}

In this Section we calculate the third term in Eq. (
41
).

\hskip 0.0pt\check{W}^{(1)}_{3\mu\nu}~=~{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle~+~x\leftrightarrow 0

Again, main contribution correspond to one index (e.g.
\mu
) being longitudinal and the other transverse so we need

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\Big({2p_{1}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\mu^{\prime}}\over s}+\mu\leftrightarrow\mu^{\prime}\Big){N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\psi_{A}(0)\big]~+~\mu^{\prime}\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~\langle A,B|p_{1\mu}\big(\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x){\not\!p}_{2}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\psi_{A}(0)\big]~+\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0){\not\!p}_{2}\psi_{A}(0)\big]\big)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~p_{2\mu}\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0){\not\!p}_{1}\psi_{A}(0)\big]|A,B\rangle{2N_{c}\over s^{2}}

(76)

(recall that
\Xi_{2}(x)~=~-{{\not\!p}_{1}\over s}\gamma^{i}A_{i}{1\over\beta}\psi_{B}(x)
so
{\not\!p}_{1}\Xi_{2}=0
).

Let us consider first the term proportional to
p_{1\mu}
.
Performing Fierz transformation (
237
) and sorting out the color-singlet contributions we get (cf. Eq. (
267
))

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{2p_{1\mu}\over s}p_{2}^{\mu^{\prime}}{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\mu^{\prime}}\psi_{A}(0)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]+\mu^{\prime}\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{1\mu}\over s^{2}}\Big\{{1\over 2}\big\{\langle\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\nu}\breve{A}_{i}(x)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\gamma^{i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{1\over s}\langle\bar{\psi}{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{\nu}(x)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!p}_{1}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}~+~(\psi(0)\otimes\psi(x)\leftrightarrow\gamma_{5}\psi(0)\otimes\gamma_{5}\psi(x)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~{1\over s}\langle\bar{\psi}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\ast\nu_{\perp}}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-~{2i\over s^{2}}\langle\bar{\psi}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\ast{\bullet}}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\sigma_{\ast\nu_{\perp}}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{i\over s}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\sigma_{\ast j}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\langle\bar{\psi}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\nu}^{~j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}+{i\over s}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\sigma_{\nu_{\perp}j}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\langle\bar{\psi}A_{i}(x)\sigma_{\ast}^{~j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\Big\}

(77)

It is clear that matrix elements in the first line in the r.h.s. can produce only transverse factors
\sim q_{\perp}
so the corresponding contribution

\sim{p_{1\mu}q^{\perp}_{\nu}m_{\perp}^{2}\over\beta_{q}s^{2}}
can be neglected. Also, matrix element

\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!p}_{1}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}
vanishes as seen from Eq. (
264
). The remaining terms can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{2p_{1\mu}\over s}p_{2}^{\mu^{\prime}}{N_{c}\over s}\langle A,B|[\bar{\psi}_{B}(0)\gamma_{\mu^{\prime}}\psi_{A}(0)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}\Xi_{2}(x)\big]+\mu^{\prime}\leftrightarrow\nu|A,B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{p_{1\mu}\over s^{3}}\Big\{-\langle\bar{\psi}{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{\nu}(x)\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0){\not\!p}_{1}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}-\langle\bar{\psi}A^{j}(x)\sigma_{\ast j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}\nu_{\perp}}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt+~\langle\bar{\psi}(x)\big[A_{\nu_{\perp}}(x)\sigma_{\ast j}-A_{j}(x)\sigma_{\ast\nu_{\perp}}\big]\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\sigma_{\bullet}^{~j}{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt+~{is\over 2}\langle\bar{\psi}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\nu}^{~j}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\big[g_{ij}-i\epsilon_{ij}\gamma_{5}-i\sigma_{ji}+{2\over s}g_{ij}\sigma_{\ast{\bullet}}\big]{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt-~i\langle\bar{\psi}A^{i}(x)\sigma_{\ast{\bullet}}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\big[g_{i\nu}-i\epsilon_{i\nu_{\perp}}\gamma_{5}+i\sigma_{i\nu_{\perp}}+{2\over s}g_{i\nu}\sigma_{\ast{\bullet}}\big]{1\over\beta}\psi(x)\rangle_{B}\Big\}

(78)

where we have used Eq. (
240
).
