{\bar{h}}_{i}(\alpha,b_{\perp})\end{array}\bigg\}~=~\int\!{d^{2}k_{\perp}\over 4\pi^{2}}~e^{i(k,b)_{\perp}}\bigg\{\begin{array}[]{c}h_{i}(\alpha,k_{\perp})\\
{\bar{h}}_{i}(\alpha,k_{\perp})\end{array}

then
W^{1H}_{\mu\nu}
can be represented as

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptm^{2}W_{\mu\nu}^{1H}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp})~=~4\pi^{2}\sum_{\rm f}e_{f}^{2}\Big(g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}h^{f}_{j}{\bar{h}}^{fj}-h^{f}_{\mu}{\bar{h}}^{f}_{\nu}-h_{\nu}{\bar{h}}^{f}_{\mu}+~2{q^{\parallel}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}-{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{4}}\partial^{i}h_{i}^{f}\partial^{j}{\bar{h}}_{j}^{f}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{q^{\parallel}_{\mu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\big[(i\partial^{i}h^{f}_{i}){\bar{h}}^{f}_{\nu}+h^{f}_{\nu}i\partial^{i}{\bar{h}}^{f}_{i}\big]+~{{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\big[(i\partial^{i}h^{f}_{i}){\bar{h}}^{f}_{\nu}-h^{f}_{\nu}i\partial^{i}{\bar{h}}^{f}_{i}\big]~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\Big)-~{{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}+q^{\parallel}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{4}}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\big[\partial_{\perp}^{2}(h^{f}_{i}{\bar{h}}_{f}^{i})+\partial^{i}h^{f}_{j}\partial^{i}{\bar{h}}^{f}_{j}\big]-~{q^{\parallel}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}+{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{4}}\partial_{i}[(h^{f}_{j}\partial{\bar{h}}_{f}^{j}-{\bar{h}}^{f}_{j}\partial h_{f}^{j})]~+~h\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}

(192)

where

h_{i}=h_{i}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})
and
{\bar{h}}_{i}\equiv{\bar{h}}_{i}(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp})
everywhere except

h\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}
terms where it is opposite, cf. Eq. (
186
).

8.2
Four Lorentz structures of hadronic tensor

The four Lorentz structures of hadronic tensor in Collins-Soper frame are given by Eq. (
2
) where (
Q_{\perp}\equiv|q_{\perp}|
)

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802ptZ~=~{{\tilde{q}}\over Q_{\parallel}}~=~{1\over Q_{\parallel}}(\alpha_{q}p_{1}-\beta_{q}p_{2}),~~~~~~~X~=~\Big[{Q_{\perp}\over Q_{\parallel}Q}(\alpha_{q}p_{1}+\beta_{q}p_{2})+{Q_{\parallel}\over Q_{\perp}Q}q_{\perp}\Big]

(193)

such that
q\cdot X=q\cdot Z=X\cdot Z=0
and
X^{2}=Z^{2}=-1
.

First, let us check the structure corresponding to the total cross section of DY pair production. From Eq. (
174
) we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\mu}_{\mu}(q)~=~-{2\over N_{c}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{\big[1-2{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over Q^{2}}\big]F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(194)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~2{k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}_{N}Q^{2}}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})+{N_{c}\over N_{c}^{2}-1}(k,q-k)_{\perp}J_{2}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})\Big\}\Big[1+O\big({1\over N_{c}^{2}}\big)\Big]+O\big({q_{\perp}^{4}\over Q^{4}}\big)

which agrees with Eq. (6.2) from Ref.
[
28
]
. This equation gives the sum of structures
W_{\mu}^{\mu}~=~-(2W_{T}+W_{L})
.

8.2.1
W_{L}

The easiest structure to get is
W_{L}
. Multiplying Eq. (
174
) by
Z_{\mu}Z_{\nu}
and comparing to Eq. (
1
) we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{L}(q)~=~Z^{\mu}Z^{\nu}W^{1}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}{1\over Q^{2}N_{c}}\!\int\!dk_{\perp}\Big\{(q-2k)_{\perp}^{2}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(195)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over m^{2}}\big(2k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-[k_{\perp}^{2}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}](k,q-k)_{\perp}\big)H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})\Big\}\Big[1+O\big({q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}\big)+O\big({1\over N_{c}^{2}}\big)\Big]

Thus, one may say that
W_{L}
is known at LHC energies at
q_{\perp}^{2}\ll Q^{2}
as far as
f_{1}
and
h_{1}^{\perp}
are known.

8.2.2
W_{\Delta}

Using formula

q_{\perp}^{\mu}Z^{\nu}W_{\mu\nu}~=~(X\cdot q)_{\perp}W_{\Delta}~=~-{Q_{\parallel}Q_{\perp}\over Q}W_{\Delta}

we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\Delta}~=~{Q\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}Q_{\perp}N_{c}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{(q,q-2k)_{\perp}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~(q,q-2k){(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over m^{2}}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})\Big\}\Big[1+O\big({q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}\big)+O\big({1\over N_{c}^{2}}\big)\Big]

(196)

Again, we see that
W_{\Delta}
is expressed via
f_{1}
and
h_{1}^{\perp}
with great accuracy.

8.2.3
W_{T}

Next, from Eqs. (
194
), (
195
) and
W_{\mu}^{\mu}~=~-(2W_{T}+W_{L})
one easily obtains

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{T}(q)~=~{1\over N_{c}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{\big[1-{q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\big]F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over 2m^{2}Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\big(2k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}+[k_{\perp}^{2}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}](k,q-k)_{\perp}\big)H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{N_{c}\over N_{c}^{2}-1}(k,q-k)_{\perp}J_{2}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})\Big\}\Big[1+O\big({1\over N_{c}^{2}}\big)\Big]+O\big({q_{\perp}^{4}\over Q^{4}}\big)

(197)

8.2.4
W_{\Delta\Delta}

Finally, the easiest way to pick out
W_{\Delta\Delta}
is to multiply
W_{\mu\nu}
by
q^{\perp}_{\mu}q^{\perp}_{\nu}/q_{\perp}^{2}
. One obtains from Eq. (
1
)

{q^{\perp}_{\mu}q^{\perp}_{\nu}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}W^{\mu\nu}(q)={Q_{\parallel}^{2}\over Q^{2}}(W_{T}-W_{\Delta\Delta})
. On the other hand, from Eqs. (
176
) and (
177
)
one gets

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{q_{\perp}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}W^{1}_{\mu\nu}(q)~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{1\over N_{c}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})+~\Big[{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over m^{2}}-{2(q,k)_{\perp}(q,q-k)_{\perp}\over m^{2}q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big]H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})\Big\}

(198)

and from Eq. (
178
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{q_{\perp}^{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}W^{2}_{\mu\nu}(q)~=~{1\over N_{c}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}{1\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}q_{\perp}^{2}}\!\int d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{{1\over m^{2}}\big\{2(q,k)_{\perp}(q,q-k)_{\perp}(k,q-k)_{\perp}

(199)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt-~k_{\perp}^{2}(q,q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-(q-k_{\perp})^{2}(q,k)_{\perp}^{2}-q_{\perp}^{2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}^{2}+q_{\perp}^{2}k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k_{\perp})^{2}\big]\big\}H_{A}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{N_{c}\over N_{c}^{2}-1}\Big([2(q,k)_{\perp}(q,q-k)_{\perp}-q_{\perp}^{2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}]J_{1}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})+~q_{\perp}^{2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}J_{2}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})\Big)\Big\}.

Thus, we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\Delta\Delta}~=~W_{T}-{Q^{2}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}{q^{\perp}_{\mu}q_{\perp}^{\nu}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}W_{\mu\nu}~=~{1\over N_{c}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{{q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q_{\parallel}^{2}}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(200)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\Big({2(q,k)_{\perp}(q,q-k)_{\perp}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}-(k,q-k)_{\perp}+{1\over Q^{2}}\big\{k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over 2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}[k_{\perp}^{2}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}]+q_{\perp}^{2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}-2(q,k)(q,q-k)_{\perp}\big\}\Big){1\over m^{2}}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over m^{2}Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\Big({k_{\perp}^{2}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}(q,q-k)_{\perp}^{2}+{(q-k_{\perp})^{2}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}(q,k)_{\perp}^{2}-k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k_{\perp})^{2}+(k,q-k)_{\perp}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 125.19212pt-~2(q,k)_{\perp}(q,q-k)_{\perp}{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big)H_{A}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{N_{c}\over N_{c}^{2}-1}{1\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\Big[(k,q-k)_{\perp}-{2(q,k)_{\perp}(q,q-k)_{\perp}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big]J_{1}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~+~O\big({1\over N_{c}^{2}}\big)\Big\}+O\big({q_{\perp}^{4}\over Q^{4}}\big)

This is the only function which has a
O\big({1\over Q^{2}}\big)
, leading-
N_{c}
contribution proportional to twist-three TMD
H_{A}
not related to leading-twist TMDs
by equations of motion. The functions
W_{T},W_{L}
, and
W_{\Delta}
do not have such contributions (although they have such contributions at the
{1\over N_{c}}
level).

8.3
Estimates of
W_{i}(q)
at
q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}

8.3.1
Order-of-magnitude estimates

Following the analysis in Ref.
[
28
]
, let us estimate the relative strength of Lorentz structures
W_{i}
at
q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}
.
First, we assume that
{1\over N_{c}}
is a good parameter and leave only terms leading in
N_{c}
.
Second, at
q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}
we probe the perturbative tails of TMD’s
f_{1}\sim{1\over k_{\perp}^{2}}
and

h_{1}^{\perp}\sim{1\over k_{\perp}^{4}}

[
45
]
.
So, as long as
Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}
we can approximate

\hskip 0.0ptf_{1}(\alpha_{z},k_{\perp}^{2})~\simeq~{f(\alpha_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}},~~h_{1}^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})~\simeq~{m^{2}_{N}h(\alpha_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{4}},~~~~~~{\bar{f}}_{1}\simeq{{\bar{f}}(\alpha_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}},~~{\bar{h}}_{1}^{\perp}\simeq{m^{2}_{N}{\bar{h}}(\alpha_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{4}}

(201)

(up to logarithmic corrections). Similarly, for the target we can use the estimate

f_{1}(\beta_{z},k_{\perp}^{2})~\simeq~{f(\beta_{z})\over k_{\perp}^{2}},~~h_{1}^{\perp}(\beta_{z},k_{\perp}^{2})~\simeq~{m^{2}_{N}h(\beta_{z})\over k_{\perp}^{4}},~~~~~~{\bar{f}}_{1}\simeq{{\bar{f}}(\beta_{z})\over k_{\perp}^{2}},~~{\bar{h}}_{1}^{\perp}\simeq{m^{2}_{N}{\bar{h}}(\beta_{z})\over k_{\perp}^{4}}

(202)

as long as
k_{\perp}^{2}\ll Q^{2}
. Thus, we get an estimate

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptF^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~\simeq~{F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}},~~~~~F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})~\equiv~f^{f}(\alpha_{q}){\bar{f}}^{f}(\beta_{q})+f^{f}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}^{f},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptH^{f}(q,k_{\perp})~\simeq~m^{4}{H^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{4}(q-k)_{\perp}^{4}},~~~~H^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})~\equiv~h^{f}(\alpha_{q}){\bar{h}}^{f}(\beta_{q})+h^{f}\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}^{f}

(203)

Note that due to the “positivity constraint”
[
46
]

h_{1}^{\perp}(x,k_{\perp}^{2})\leq{m\over|k_{\perp}|}f_{1}^{\perp}(x,k_{\perp}^{2})~~~

(204)

we can safely assume that the functions
f(x)
and
h(x)
defined in
Eqs. (
201
) and (
202
) are of the same order of magnitude.
Moreover, both theoretical
[
47
]
and phenomenological
[
48
,
49
]
analysis indicate that
h_{1}^{\perp}
is several times smaller than
f_{1}

so in numerical estimates we will disregard the contribution of
h_{1}^{\perp}
.

8.3.2
Power corrections for total DY cross section

Substituting the above approximations to Eq. (
194
) we get
the following estimate of the strength of power corrections for total DY cross section
[
28
]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{\mu}_{\mu}(q)~=~-{2\over N_{c}}\sum e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{\Big[1-2{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over Q^{2}}\Big]F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})+~2{k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}_{N}Q^{2}}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\simeq~-{2\over N_{c}}\sum e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{\Big[1-2{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over Q^{2}}\Big]{F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}+{2m^{2}\over Q^{2}}{H^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\simeq~-{2\over N_{c}}\sum e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big[1-2{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over Q^{2}}\Big]{F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}

(205)

where we used estimates (
203
) and the fact that
(k,q-k)_{\perp}\sim q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}
.
Thus, the relative weight of the leading term and power correction is determined by the factor

1-2{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over Q^{2}}
. Due to Eqs. (
201
) and (
202
), the integrals over
k_{\perp}
are logarithmic and should be cut from below
by
m^{2}_{N}
and from above by
Q^{2}
so we get an estimate

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}{1\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}~\simeq~{2\pi\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}},~~~~~~\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}~\simeq~-\pi\ln{Q^{2}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}

(206)

where we assumed that the first integral is determined by the logarithmical region
q_{\perp}^{2}\gg k_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}_{N}
and the second by
Q^{2}\gg k_{\perp}^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
.
Taking these integrals to Eq. (
205
) one obtains

\hskip 0.0ptW_{\mu}^{\mu}(q)~=~-{4\pi\over N_{c}}\sum e_{f}^{2}\Big[{1\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}_{N}}+{1\over Q^{2}}\ln{Q^{2}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big]F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})

(207)

By this estimate, the power correction reaches the level of few percent at
q_{\perp}\sim{Q\over 4}
.

8.3.3
Power corrections for
W_{T}

Let us now consider estimates described in Sect.
8.3.1
for
W_{T}
given by Eq. (
197
).
At large
N_{c}
, we can omit the third line so

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptW_{T}(q)~=~{1\over N_{c}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!{\textstyle d}\lower 0.12915pt\hbox{\kern-3.80005pt${}^{\scriptstyle-}$}\kern-0.50003pt{}^{2}k_{\perp}\Big[1-{q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q^{2}}\Big]F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

(208)

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~{1\over 2m^{2}Q^{2}}\big(2k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}+[k_{\perp}^{2}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}](k,q-k)_{\perp}\big)H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\simeq~{1\over N_{c}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!{\textstyle d}\lower 0.12915pt\hbox{\kern-3.80005pt${}^{\scriptstyle-}$}\kern-0.50003pt{}^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{\Big[1-{q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q^{2}}\Big]{F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~{m^{2}\over Q^{2}}\Big(1+[k_{\perp}^{2}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}]{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over 2k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}\Big){H^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}\Big\}

(209)

Again, due to
q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}
the second term in braces can be neglected and we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{T}(q)~\simeq~{2\pi\over N_{c}}\Big[{1\over q_{\perp}^{2}}-{1\over 2Q^{2}}\Big]\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}\sum e_{f}^{2}F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})

(210)

Thus, for
W_{T}
the power correction reaches 10% level at
q_{\perp}\sim{Q\over 2}
.

8.3.4
Estimate of
W_{L}

Again, using estimates from Sect.
8.3.1
one obtains

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{L}(q)~=~\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}{1\over Q^{2}N_{c}}\!\int\!{dk_{\perp}\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}\Big\{(q-2k)_{\perp}^{2}F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~m^{2}\Big(2-[k_{\perp}^{2}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}]{(k,q-k)_{\perp}\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}}\Big)H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})\Big\}

(211)

which gives approximately

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{L}(q)~\simeq~{2\pi\over Q^{2}N_{c}}\Big[\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}_{N}}+2\ln{Q^{2}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big]\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})

(212)

in agreement with Eqs. (
207
) and (
210
).
The estimate of the ratio of
W_{L}/W_{T}
is

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{W_{L}(q)\over W_{T}(q)}~\simeq~{q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}\Big[1+2{\ln Q^{2}/q_{\perp}^{2}\over\ln q_{\perp}^{2}/m^{2}}\Big]

(213)

8.3.5
Magnitude of
W_{\Delta}

It is easy to see that
W_{\Delta}
vanishes if one uses the estimates (
201
) and (
202
). Indeed, with these formulas

F(q,k_{\perp})
and
H(q,k_{\perp})
are symmetric under replacement
k_{\perp}\leftrightarrow(q-k)_{\perp}
whereas
(q,q-2k)_{\perp}
in
the integrand in Eq. (
196
) is antisymmetric. Moreover, this vanishing of
W_{\Delta}
will occur for any factorizable model of TMDs
f_{1}
and
h_{1}^{\perp}
:
if
f_{1}(\alpha,k_{\perp})~=~f(\alpha)\phi(k_{\perp})
and
h_{1}^{\perp}(\alpha,k_{\perp})~=~h(\alpha)\psi(k_{\perp})
the integral (
196
)
vanishes.
On the other hand,
W_{\Delta}
is only
\sim{Q_{\perp}\over Q}W_{T}
so without better knowledge of TMDs it is impossible
to tell whether
W_{\Delta}
is smaller or bigger than, say,
W_{L}
. Also, if the parameter
\alpha_{q}{Q\over Q_{\perp}}
is not negligible, to compare

W_{\Delta}
and
W_{L}
one needs to take into account
O(\alpha_{q})

corrections to
W_{\Delta}
defined by TMDs other than
f_{1}
and
h_{1}^{\perp}
.

8.3.6
Estimate of
W_{\Delta\Delta}

Let us consider the relative weight of the terms in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
200
). As we mentioned, we assume that
{1\over N_{c}}
is a valid
small parameter so we can omit the last
J_{1}
term. Also, it is natural to assume that
H_{A}^{f}(q,k_{\perp})
is of the same order of magnitude as

H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})
and, since the term with
H_{A}
is a power correction, it is not unreasonable to neglect this term in the first approximation.
Using now estimates (
203
) and the integrals

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\int\!d^{2}k{k_{i}(q-k)_{j}\over k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{4}}\theta(k_{\perp}^{2}-m^{2})\theta((q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-m^{2})~\simeq~{\pi\over 2q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big(g^{\perp}_{ik}+2{q_{i}q_{k}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big)\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\int\!d^{2}k{k_{i}(q-k)_{j}\over k_{\perp}^{4}(q-k)_{\perp}^{4}}\theta(k_{\perp}^{2}-m^{2})\theta((q-k)_{\perp}^{2}-m^{2})~\simeq~{\pi\over q_{\perp}^{4}}\Big(g^{\perp}_{ik}+4{q_{i}q_{k}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}\Big)\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}

(214)

one gets an estimate

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\Delta\Delta}~\simeq~{1\over N_{c}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\!\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}\Big\{{q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q_{\parallel}^{2}}F^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\Big({2(q,k)_{\perp}(q,q-k)_{\perp}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}-(k,q-k)_{\perp}+{1\over Q^{2}}\big[k_{\perp}^{2}(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over 2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}[k_{\perp}^{2}+(q-k)_{\perp}^{2}]+q_{\perp}^{2}(k,q-k)_{\perp}-2(q,k)(q,q-k)_{\perp}\big]\Big){1\over m^{2}}H^{f}(q,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\simeq~{\pi\over Q^{2}N_{c}}\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}\over m^{2}}\sum_{f}e_{f}^{2}\Big[F^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})+{4m^{2}Q^{2}\over q_{\perp}^{4}}\Big(1-{q_{\perp}^{2}\over 2Q^{2}}\Big)H^{f}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})\Big]

(215)

8.3.7
Lam-Tung relation

It is easy to see that if one neglects
H
in Eq. (
195
) the ratio of
W_{L}
and
2W_{\Delta\Delta}
is approximately

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{W_{L}\over 2W_{\Delta\Delta}}~\simeq~1+2{\ln Q^{2}/q_{\perp}^{2}\over\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}/m^{2}}}

(216)

It seems like the Lam-Tung relation works better if we move closer to the domain of collinear factorization
Q^{2}\sim Q_{\perp}^{2}\gg m^{2}
.

8.3.8
Estimates of asymmetries

The differential cross section of DY process is parametrized as

\hskip 2.84526pt\Big({d\sigma\over d^{4}q}\Big)^{-1}{d\sigma\over d\Omega d^{4}q}~=~{3\over 4\pi(\lambda+3)}\big(1+\lambda\cos^{2}\theta+\mu\sin 2\theta\cos\phi+{\nu\over 2}\sin^{2}\theta\cos 2\phi\big)

(217)

where
\Omega

is the solid angle of the lepton in terms of its polar and azimuthal angles in the center-of-mass system of the lepton pair.
The angular coefficients
\lambda
,
\mu
, and
\nu
can be expressed in terms of the hadronic tensor:

\hskip 2.84526pt\lambda~=~{W_{T}-W_{L}\over W_{T}+W_{L}},~~~\mu~=~{W_{\Delta}\over W_{T}+W_{L}},~~~\nu~=~{2W_{\Delta\Delta}\over W_{T}+W_{L}}

(218)

For an estimate, let us take
s
=8 TeV and
Q
=90 GeV so that
x_{A}\sim x_{B}\sim 0.1
in central region of rapidity.
Although we did not include the contribution of
Z
-boson, we can compare our order-of-magnitude estimates with experimental data
at this kinematics
[
50
,
51
]
.
Let us take
Q_{\perp}
= 20 GeV so
the power corrections
\sim{Q_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
are small but sizable, of order of few per cent. At this kinematics, we obtain

\hskip 2.84526pt1-\lambda~=~2{W_{L}\over W_{T}+W_{L}}~\simeq~2{1+2{\ln Q^{2}/q_{\perp}^{2}\over\ln q_{\perp}^{2}/m^{2}}\over{Q^{2}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}-{1\over 2}+2{\ln Q^{2}/q_{\perp}^{2}\over\ln q_{\perp}^{2}/m^{2}}}~\simeq~0.19

(219)

from Eq. (
212
) which agrees with estimates in Ref.
[
52
]
. Next, in our kinematics the expression
in square brackets in the r.h.s. in Eq. (
215
) is approximately
F+0.17H
. Since the Boer-Mulders function
seem to be of order of few percent of
f_{1}
(see the discussion in Sect.
8.3.1
), the term with
H
can be safely neglected and we get

\hskip 2.84526pt\nu~=~{2W_{\Delta\Delta}\over W_{T}+W_{L}}~\simeq~{1\over{Q^{2}\over q_{\perp}^{2}}-{1\over 2}+2{\ln Q^{2}/q_{\perp}^{2}\over\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}/m^{2}}}}~\simeq~0.05

(220)

As to
\mu
coefficient, as we mentioned, we cannot estimate it since with factorization hypothesis
for TMDs it vanishes. Reversing the argument, if
\mu
will be measured to be much smaller than
\nu
, it will be an argument in favor
of factorization hypothesis for TMDs
f_{1}
and
h_{1}^{\perp}
. Actually, there are experiments at much lower
q_{\perp}
and
Q

\sim
few GeV
which indicate that
\mu
is very small
[
53
]
.

Last but not least, let us estimate Lam-Tung relation. With our approximation in the above kinematics we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{W_{L}\over 2W_{\Delta\Delta}}~\simeq~1+2{\ln Q^{2}/q_{\perp}^{2}\over\ln{q_{\perp}^{2}/m^{2}}}~\simeq~2.0

(221)

so it seems to be violated at this kinematics. Again, these order-of-magnitude estimates do not include the contribution to DY cross section
mediated by the
Z
-boson.

9
Coefficient functions and matching of rapidity cutoffs

The result (
175
) is a tree-level formula and to fully understand Eq. (
3
) we
should specify the rapidity cutoffs for
f_{1}
’s and
h_{1}^{\perp}
’s. As we discussed in section
3
,
the rapidity cutoff for longitudinal momenta in
f_{1}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})
is
\beta\leq\sigma_{p}
and for
f_{1}(\beta_{q},k_{\perp})

\alpha\leq\sigma_{t}
,
where
\sigma_{p}
and
\sigma_{t}
are rapidity bounds for central fields. To avoid double counting, the region where both

\alpha<\sigma_{t}
and
\beta<\sigma_{p}
should give only small power corrections. This is achieved if one takes

\sigma_{p},\sigma_{t}\sim{Q_{\perp}\over\sqrt{s}}
so power corrections from double counting are
{Q_{\perp}\over Q}
.
In this case, the region
\alpha_{q}>\alpha>\sigma_{t},~\beta_{q}>\beta>\sigma_{p}
gives Sudakov double-log
factor

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptC\Big(q,k,{\alpha_{q}\over\sigma_{t}},{\beta_{q}\over\sigma_{p}}\Big)~\sim~e^{-{\alpha_{s}c_{F}\over\pi}\ln{\alpha_{q}\over\sigma_{t}}\ln{\beta_{q}\over\sigma_{p}}}

(222)

where the coefficient
{\alpha_{s}c_{F}\over\pi}
is two times
\gamma_{\rm cusp}
for quarks.
A more precise formula can be obtained from the requirement
that the product of two TMDs and the coefficient function (
222
) does not depend on the “rapidity divides”

\sigma_{p}
and
\sigma_{t}
. For simplicity, let us start with the leading-twist term
\sim g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}F
.
Rapidity evolution of the function
f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p})
was found in Ref.
[
54
]

13
13

13

As noted in Ref.
[
54
]
, the factor
\sim\gamma_{E}
depends on the exact way to cut integrals over
\alpha

and
\beta
. Here the factor
-\gamma_{E}
corresponds to “smooth” cutoffs
e^{-{\alpha\over\sigma_{t}}}
and
e^{-{\beta\over\sigma_{p}}}
,
see the discussion in Ref.
[
54
]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{d\over d\ln\varsigma_{p}}f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p})~=~{\alpha_{s}C_{F}\over\pi}\big[-\ln\alpha_{q}\varsigma_{p}-{1\over 2}\ln{b_{\perp}^{2}s\over 4}-\gamma_{E}+O(\alpha_{s})\big]f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{d\over d\ln\varsigma_{t}}f_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})~=~{\alpha_{s}C_{F}\over\pi}\big[-\ln\beta_{q}\varsigma_{t}-{1\over 2}\ln{b_{\perp}^{2}s\over 4}-\gamma_{E}+O(\alpha_{s})\big]f_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})

(223)

where
\varsigma_{p}=\sigma_{p}b_{\perp}\sqrt{s}
,
\varsigma_{t}=\sigma_{t}b_{\perp}\sqrt{s}
are
b_{\perp}
-dependent cutoffs providing conformal invariance of the
leading-order TMD rapidity evolution (in the coordinate space) and
\gamma_{E}
is Euler’s constant.
Similar equation holds true for
{\bar{f}}_{1}
since it is obtained from the evolution of the same operator.

Looking at Eqs. (
222
) and (
223
) one can guess that
the coefficient function
\sim g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}
times two TMDs
f_{1}
in the coordinate space has the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{g_{\perp}}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp})~\sim~M(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p},\varsigma_{t})\big[f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p}){\bar{f}}_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})+f_{1}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}_{1}\big]

(224)

where

\hskip 0.0ptM(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p},\varsigma_{t})~=~e^{-{\alpha_{s}c_{F}\over\pi}\ln\big({\alpha_{q}b_{\perp}\sqrt{s}\over\varsigma_{t}}e^{\gamma_{E}}\big)\ln\big({\beta_{q}b_{\perp}\sqrt{s}\over\varsigma_{p}}e^{\gamma_{E}}\big)+{\alpha_{s}c_{F}\over 2\pi}\ln^{2}\varsigma_{p}\varsigma_{t}}\big[1+O(\alpha_{s})\big]

(225)

It is easy to check that with
M
given by Eq. (
225
) we have
{d\over d\varsigma_{p}}
(r.h.s. of Eq. (
224
)) = 0
and
{d\over d\varsigma_{t}}
(r.h.s. of Eq. (
224
)) = 0 so our guess (
225
) for the coefficient function
is correct up to
O(\alpha_{s})
terms.

14
14

14

The Eq. (
225
) is obtained in the leading order in
\alpha_{s}
so
the argument of coupling constant is left undetermined.
One should expect Sudakov formula with running coupling constant
[
55
,
11
]
at the NLO level.

To write precise matching for other parts of
W_{\mu\nu}
is a more complicated task. Let us start with
W^{1F}_{\mu\nu}
terms considered in
the next Section.

9.1
Matching for
W^{1F}
terms

We need to multiply Eq. (
186
) in coordinate space by
M(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p},\varsigma_{t})
.
First, recall that

\hskip 0.0ptM(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p},\varsigma_{t})[f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p}){\bar{f}}_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})+f_{1}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}_{1}\big]

(226)

does not actually depend on the “rapidity divides”
\varsigma_{p}
and
\varsigma_{t}
. However, the differentiation

{\partial\over\partial b_{i}}
affects evolution equations (
223
).
In this case we modify the derivative with respect to
b_{i}
as follows

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\tilde{\partial}_{i}f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p})~\equiv~\big(\partial_{i}-{\alpha_{s}c_{F}\over\pi}{b^{i}\over b_{\perp}^{2}}\ln\varsigma_{p}\big)f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\tilde{\partial}_{i}f_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})~\equiv~\big(\partial_{i}-{\alpha_{s}c_{F}\over\pi}{b^{i}\over b_{\perp}^{2}}\ln\varsigma_{t}\big)f_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})

(227)

(and similarly for
{\bar{f}}
’s)
so that the l.h.s.’ of these equations satisfy Eqs. (
223
).

15
15

15

Strictly speaking, the difference between
\tilde{\partial}_{i}f
and
\partial_{i}f
is

\sim O(\alpha_{s})
but since our matching is correct at single-log limit (see Eq. (
225
)) we keep
\tilde{\partial}_{i}f

to avoid corrections
\sim O(\alpha_{s}\ln\varsigma)
in our matching formulas (
186
) (and (
192
) below).

Note also that
\partial_{i}(Mf{\bar{f}})=M(f\tilde{\partial}_{i}{\bar{f}}+{\bar{f}}\tilde{\partial}_{i}f)
.
With this definitions, one can write
W^{1F}_{\mu\nu}
in the double-log approximation in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{1F}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp})~=~\sum_{\rm flavors}e_{f}^{2}W^{fF}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp})~

(228)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW^{fF}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp})~=

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=~4\pi^{2}\Big\{-g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}+{i\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}(q^{\parallel}_{\mu}\partial^{\perp}_{\nu}+q^{\parallel}_{\nu}\partial^{\perp}_{\mu})-{q^{\parallel}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}+{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\partial_{\perp}^{2}\Big]Mf_{1}{\bar{f}}_{1}-~{4{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}M(\tilde{\partial}^{i}f_{1}^{f})(\tilde{\partial}^{i}{\bar{f}}_{1}^{f})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-~\Big[{{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\Big(\delta_{\nu}^{i}-{q^{\parallel}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}i\partial^{i}\Big)M(f_{1}\tilde{\partial}_{i}{\bar{f}}_{1}^{f}-{\bar{f}}_{1}\tilde{\partial}_{i}f_{1}^{f})+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\Big]+f_{1}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}_{1}\Big\}+O(\alpha_{s})

where
M=M(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p},\varsigma_{t})
and

f_{1}=f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp},\varsigma_{p})
,
{\bar{f}}_{1}\equiv{\bar{f}}_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp},\varsigma_{t})
everywhere except

f\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}
terms where it is opposite.

It is easy to check gauge invariance:
(\alpha_{q}p_{1}^{\mu}+\beta_{q}p_{2}^{\mu}+i\partial_{\perp}^{\mu})W_{\mu\nu}^{1F}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp})~=~0
.

9.2
Matching for
W^{1H}
terms

First, with our definitions (
8.1
) the Eq. (
265
) reads

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\sigma_{i{\bullet}}\psi_{f}(0)|A\rangle=~{1\over m_{N}}h_{i}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(0)\sigma_{i{\bullet}}\psi_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle=~-{1\over m_{N}}{\bar{h}}_{i}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

(229)

and similarly for the target matrix elements. With such definition, the evolution equation for

h_{i}(\alpha,b_{\perp})\equiv{1\over 4\pi^{2}}\int\!d^{2}k_{\perp}e^{i(k,x)_{\perp}}h_{i}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

is the same as Eq. (
223
)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{d\over d\ln\varsigma_{p}}h_{i}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p})~=~{\alpha_{s}C_{F}\over\pi}\big[-\ln\alpha_{q}\varsigma_{p}-\ln{|b_{\perp}|\sqrt{s}\over 2}-\gamma_{E}\big]h_{i}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{d\over d\ln\varsigma_{t}}h_{i}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})~=~{\alpha_{s}C_{F}\over\pi}\big[-\ln\beta_{q}\varsigma_{t}-{1\over 2}\ln{b_{\perp}^{2}s\over 4}-\gamma_{E}\big]h_{i}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})

(230)

and similarly for
{\bar{h}}_{i}
. The reason is that
one-loop rapidity evolution for
\hat{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Gamma{\hat{\psi}}(0)
in the
Sudakov region is the same for all matrices
\Gamma
between
\hat{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})

and
{\hat{\psi}}_{(}0)
due to the fact that the “handbag” diagram in Fig.
3
c is small
and in two other diagrams (as well as self-energy corrections) the matrix
\Gamma
between
\hat{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})

and
{\hat{\psi}}(0)
just multiplies the result of calculation.

Figure 3:
Typical diagrams for the rapidity evolution of quark TMD in the Sudakov regime.

Next, to write down the product of
m^{2}W_{\mu\nu}^{H}
and the coefficient function
we need modified derivatives of
h_{i}
’s of Eq. (
227
) type:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\tilde{\partial}_{i}h_{j}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p})~\equiv~\big(\partial_{i}-{\alpha_{s}c_{F}\over\pi}{b^{i}\over b_{\perp}^{2}}\ln\varsigma_{p}\big)h_{j}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\tilde{\partial}_{i}h_{j}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})~\equiv~\big(\partial_{i}-{\alpha_{s}c_{F}\over\pi}{b^{i}\over b_{\perp}^{2}}\ln\varsigma_{t}\big)h_{j}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})

(231)

(and similarly for
{\bar{h}}
’s) so that
\tilde{\partial}_{i}h_{j}
will satisfy same evolution equations (
230
) as
h_{j}
. We get then

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptW_{\mu\nu}^{1H}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp})~=~\sum_{\rm flavors}e_{f}^{2}W_{\mu\nu}^{fH}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{m^{2}\over 4\pi^{2}}W_{\mu\nu}^{fH}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp})~=~M\Big(g_{\mu\nu}^{\perp}h^{f}_{j}{\bar{h}}^{fj}-h^{f}_{\mu}{\bar{h}}^{f}_{\nu}-h_{\nu}{\bar{h}}^{f}_{\mu}+~2{q^{\parallel}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}-{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{4}}\tilde{\partial}^{i}h_{i}^{f}\tilde{\partial}^{j}{\bar{h}}_{j}^{f}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{q^{\parallel}_{\mu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\big[(i\tilde{\partial}^{i}h^{f}_{i}){\bar{h}}^{f}_{\nu}+h^{f}_{\nu}i\tilde{\partial}^{i}{\bar{h}}^{f}_{i}\big]+~{{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{2}}\big[(i\tilde{\partial}^{i}h^{f}_{i}){\bar{h}}^{f}_{\nu}-h^{f}_{\nu}i\tilde{\partial}^{i}{\bar{h}}^{f}_{i}\big]~+~\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\Big)-~{{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}+q^{\parallel}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{4}}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\times~\big[\partial_{\perp}^{2}(Mh^{f}_{i}{\bar{h}}_{f}^{i})+M\tilde{\partial}^{i}h^{f}_{j}\tilde{\partial}^{i}{\bar{h}}^{f}_{j}\big]-~{q^{\parallel}_{\mu}{\tilde{q}}_{\nu}+{\tilde{q}}_{\mu}q^{\parallel}_{\nu}\over Q_{\parallel}^{4}}\partial_{i}[M(h^{f}_{j}\tilde{\partial}{\bar{h}}_{f}^{j}-{\bar{h}}^{f}_{j}\tilde{\partial}h_{f}^{j})]~+~h\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}

(232)

where
M=M(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q};\varsigma_{p},\varsigma_{t})
and

h_{i}=h_{i}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp},\varsigma_{p})
,
{\bar{h}}_{i}\equiv{\bar{h}}_{i}(\beta_{q},(q-k)_{\perp},\varsigma_{t})
everywhere except

h\leftrightarrow{\bar{h}}
terms where it is opposite, cf. Eq. (
186
).

Let us comment on the choice of “rapidity divides”
\varsigma_{p}
and
\varsigma_{t}
in the product

M(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p},\varsigma_{t})f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{p}){\bar{f}}_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\varsigma_{t})
(and in similar
Mh{\bar{h}}
product).
As we mentioned in the beginning of this Section, in order to avoid double counting one should write down factorization of the amplitude in projectile, target and central fields at
\varsigma_{p},\varsigma_{t}\sim 1
. After that, as discussed in Ref.
[
54
]
,
one can use the double-log Sudakov evolution (
223
) until

\varsigma_{p}\geq\check{\varsigma}_{p},~~~~\check{\varsigma}_{p}\equiv{1\over\alpha_{q}b_{\perp}\sqrt{s}},~~~~~~~~~\varsigma_{t}\geq\check{\varsigma}_{t},~~~~\check{\varsigma}_{t}\equiv{1\over\beta_{q}b_{\perp}\sqrt{s}}

(233)

At this point, the result of Sudakov evolution is

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptM(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\check{\varsigma}_{p},\check{\varsigma}_{t})\big[f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\check{\varsigma}_{p}){\bar{f}}_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\check{\varsigma}_{t})+f_{1}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~e^{-{\alpha_{s}c_{F}\over 2\pi}\ln^{2}{Q^{2}b_{\perp}^{2}}}\big[f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\check{\varsigma}_{p}){\bar{f}}_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\check{\varsigma}_{t})+f_{1}\leftrightarrow{\bar{f}}_{1}\big]

(234)

so in the final result (
186
) one should take

Mf{\bar{f}}~\rightarrow~e^{-{\alpha_{s}N_{c}\over 2\pi}\ln^{2}{Q^{2}b_{\perp}^{2}}}\Big[1+O\big(\alpha_{s}\big)\Big]f_{1}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\check{\varsigma}_{p}){\bar{f}}_{1}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\check{\varsigma}_{t})

(235)

Similarly, for
W_{\mu\nu}^{1H}(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q},b_{\perp})
one should take

Mh_{i}{\bar{h}}_{j}~\rightarrow~e^{-{\alpha_{s}N_{c}\over 2\pi}\ln^{2}{Q^{2}b_{\perp}^{2}}}\Big[1+O\big(\alpha_{s}\big)\Big]h_{i}(\alpha_{q},b_{\perp};\check{\varsigma}_{p}){\bar{h}}_{j}(\beta_{q},b_{\perp};\check{\varsigma}_{t})

(236)

at the end of Sudakov evolution (
230
). It should be emphasized that since factor
M
is universal for (
235
) and (
236
),
our estimates of asymmetries in Sect.
8.3.8
are not affected by summation of Sudakov double logs.

9.2.1
Rapidity-only cutoff for TMD

As discussed in Refs.
[
29
,
28
]
, from the rapidity factorization (
14
) we get
TMDs with rapidity-only cutoff
|\alpha|<\sigma_{t}
or
|\beta|<\sigma_{p}
(or with modifications (
233
)). Such cutoff, relevant for small-
x
physics, is
different from the combination of UV and rapidity cutoffs for TMDs used by moderate-
x
community, see the analysis in two
[
56
,
57
,
58
]

and three
[
59
]
loops.
For the tree-level formulas of Sect.
8
, this difference in cutoffs does not matter, but if one uses the formulas from Sect.
9

and integrates models for TMDs with Sudakov factor
M
of Eq. (
225
), one has to relate TMDs with rapidity-only cutoffs
to the TMD models with conventional cutoffs.
This requires calculations at the NLO level which are in progress.

10
Conclusions and outlook

Main result of this paper is Eq. (
174
) which gives the DY hadronic tensor for electromagnetic current at small
x

with gauge invariance at the
{1\over Q^{2}}
level.
The part (
175
), determined by leading-twist TMDs
f_{1}
and
h_{1}^{\perp}
, is manifestly gauge invariant.
The only non-gauge invariant term at the
{1\over Q^{2}}
level is Eq. (
179
) with transverse
\mu
and
\nu

which is
\sim{q^{\perp}_{\mu}q^{\perp}_{\nu}\over Q^{2}}
times twist-3 TMDs. Also, in the leading-
N_{c}
approximation
the only structure affected by those terms is
W_{\Delta\Delta}
, all other structures are calculated up to
O\big({q_{\perp}^{4}\over Q^{4}}\big)
terms.
It is interesting to note that
{1\over Q^{2}}
terms necessary for gauge invariance are calculated more than than two decades after
the calculation of
{1\over Q}
corrections in Ref.
[
20
]
.

It should be emphasized that, as discussed above, our rapidity factorization is different
from the standard factorization scheme for particle production in hadron-hadron
scattering, namely splitting the diagrams in collinear to projectile part, collinear to
target part, hard factor, and soft factor
[
9
]
. Here we factorize only in rapidity
and the
Q^{2}
evolution arises from
k_{\perp}^{2}
dependence of the rapidity evolution kernels, same
as in the BK (and NLO BK
[
60
]
) equations. Also, since matrix elements of TMD operators
with our rapidity cutoffs are UV-finite
[
61
,
62
]
, the only UV divergencies
in our approach are usual
UV divergencies absorbed in the QCD running coupling. For the tree-level result (
174
) this does not matter,
but if one intends to use the result like (
186
) with Sudakov logarithms for conventional TMDs with double
UV and rapidity cutoffs, one needs to relate our TMDs with rapidity-only cutoff to conventional TMDs.
Needless to say,
the gauge-invariant tree-level result (
175
) should be correct for TMDs with any cutoffs.

An obvious outlook is to extend these results to the “real” DY process involving
Z
-boson contributions which are relevant for
our kinematics.
Another outlook is the one-loop calculations in this rapidity-based factorization and comparison to resummations
of large
\ln x
and
\ln{Q^{2}/Q_{\perp}^{2}}
based on usual
collinear factorization, see e.g. Refs.
[
63
,
34
]
. The study is in progress.

The author is grateful to V. Braun, A. Prokudin, A. Radyushkin, J. Qiu, and A. Vladimirov for valuable discussions. This work is
supported by Jefferson Science Associates, LLC under the U.S. DOE contract #DE-AC05-06OR23177
and by U.S. DOE grant #DE-FG02-97ER41028.

11
Appendix

11.1
Formulas with Dirac matrices

11.1.1
Fierz transformation

First, let us write down Fierz transformation for symmetric hadronic tensor

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 2}[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\mu}\chi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\nu}\psi)+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu]

(237)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{1\over 4}\big(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\big)\big[({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\alpha}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\beta}\chi)+({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{\alpha}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{\beta}\gamma_{5}\chi)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~{1\over 4}\big(\delta_{\mu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\nu}^{\beta}+\delta_{\nu}^{\alpha}\delta_{\mu}^{\beta}-{1\over 2}g_{\mu\nu}g^{\alpha\beta}\big)({\bar{\psi}}\sigma_{\alpha\xi}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\sigma_{\beta}^{~\xi}\chi)-{g_{\mu\nu}\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\chi)+{g_{\mu\nu}\over 4}({\bar{\psi}}\gamma_{5}\psi)({\bar{\chi}}\gamma_{5}\chi)

11.1.2
Formulas with
\sigma
-matrices

It is convenient to define

16
16

16

We use conventions from
Bjorken & Drell
where
\epsilon^{0123}=-1
and

\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{\lambda}=g^{\mu\nu}\gamma^{\lambda}+g^{\nu\lambda}\gamma^{\mu}-g^{\mu\lambda}\gamma^{\nu}-i\epsilon^{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}\gamma_{\rho}\gamma_{5}
.
Also, with this convention
\tilde{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}\equiv{1\over 2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}\sigma^{\lambda\rho}=i\sigma_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}
.

\epsilon_{ij}~\equiv~{2\over s}\epsilon_{\ast{\bullet}ij}~=~{2\over s}p_{2}^{\mu}p_{1}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\mu\nu ij}

(238)

such that
\epsilon_{12}~=~1
and
\epsilon_{ij}\epsilon_{kl}~=~g_{ik}g_{jl}-g_{il}g_{jk}
.
The frequently used formula is

\hskip-2.84526pt\sigma_{\mu\nu}\sigma_{\alpha\beta}~=~(g_{\mu\alpha}g_{\nu\beta}-g_{\mu\beta}g_{\nu\alpha})-i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\gamma_{5}-i(g_{\mu\alpha}\sigma_{\nu\beta}-g_{\mu\beta}\sigma_{\nu\alpha}-g_{\nu\alpha}\sigma_{\mu\beta}+g_{\nu\beta}\sigma_{\mu\alpha})

(239)

with variations

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{2\over s}\sigma_{{\bullet}i}\sigma_{\ast j}~=~g_{ij}-i\epsilon_{ij}\gamma_{5}-i\sigma_{ij}-{2i\over s}g_{ij}\sigma_{{\bullet}\ast},~~~{2\over s}\sigma_{\ast i}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}~=~g_{ij}+i\epsilon_{ij}\gamma_{5}-i\sigma_{ij}+{2\over s}g_{ij}\sigma_{\ast{\bullet}},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\sigma_{ij}\sigma_{{\bullet}k}=-\sigma_{{\bullet}k}\sigma_{ij}~=-ig_{ik}\sigma_{{\bullet}j}+ig_{jk}\sigma_{{\bullet}i},~~~\sigma_{ij}\sigma_{\ast k}=-\sigma_{\ast k}\sigma_{ij}~=-ig_{ik}\sigma_{\ast j}+ig_{jk}\sigma_{\ast i}

(240)

We need also the following formulas with
\sigma
-matrices in different matrix elements

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\tilde{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}\otimes\tilde{\sigma}_{\alpha\beta}~=~-{1\over 2}(g_{\mu\alpha}g_{\nu\beta}-g_{\nu\alpha}g_{\mu\beta})\sigma_{\xi\eta}\otimes\sigma^{\xi\eta}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~g_{\mu\alpha}\sigma_{\beta\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}-g_{\nu\alpha}\sigma_{\beta\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\mu}^{~\xi}-g_{\mu\beta}\sigma_{\alpha\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}+g_{\nu\beta}\sigma_{\alpha\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\mu}^{~\xi}-\sigma_{\alpha\beta}\otimes\sigma_{\mu\nu}

(241)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\tilde{\sigma}_{\mu\xi}\otimes\tilde{\sigma}_{\nu}^{~\xi}~=~-{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2}\sigma_{\xi\eta}\otimes\sigma^{\xi\eta}+\sigma_{\nu\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\mu}^{~\xi},~~~~\sigma_{\xi\eta}\otimes\tilde{\sigma}^{\xi\eta}~=~\tilde{\sigma}_{\xi\eta}\otimes\sigma^{\xi\eta}

(242)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\sigma_{\mu\xi}\gamma_{5}\otimes\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}\gamma_{5}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu-{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2}\sigma_{\xi\eta}\gamma_{5}\otimes\sigma^{\xi\eta}\gamma_{5}~=~-[\sigma_{\mu\xi}\otimes\sigma_{\nu}^{~\xi}+\mu\leftrightarrow\nu-{g_{\mu\nu}\over 2}\sigma_{\xi\eta}\otimes\sigma^{\xi\eta}]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\sigma_{\ast}^{~k}\otimes\gamma_{i}\sigma_{{\bullet}k}\gamma_{j}~=~{\hat{p}}_{2}\gamma^{k}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}\gamma_{k}\gamma_{j}~=~{\hat{p}}_{2}\gamma^{k}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}(g_{ik}\gamma_{j}+g_{jk}\gamma_{i}-g_{ij}\gamma_{k})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{\hat{p}}_{2}(g_{ik}\gamma_{j}+g_{jk}\gamma_{i}-g_{ij}\gamma_{k})\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{k}~=~(\gamma_{j}\sigma_{\ast}^{~k}\gamma_{i})\otimes\sigma_{{\bullet}k}

(243)

We will need also

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{j}+{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{j}\gamma_{5}~=~\gamma_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}+\gamma_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}

(244)

11.1.3
Formulas with
\gamma
-matrices and one gluon field

In the gauge
A_{\bullet}=0
the field
A_{i}
can be represented as

A_{i}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~=~{2\over s}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}~A_{\ast i}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp})

(245)

(see eq. (
17
)). We define “dual” fields by

\tilde{A}_{i}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~=~{2\over s}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}~\tilde{A}_{\ast i}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp}),~~~\tilde{B}_{i}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})~=~{2\over s}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\ast}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\ast}~\tilde{B}_{{\bullet}i}(x^{\prime}_{\ast},x_{\perp}),

(246)

where
{\tilde{F}}_{\mu\nu}={1\over 2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\lambda\rho}F^{\lambda\rho}
as usual.
With this definition we have
{\tilde{A}}_{i}=-\epsilon_{ij}A^{j}
and
{\tilde{B}}_{i}=\epsilon_{ij}B^{j}
so

{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{i}~=~-{\not\!\!A}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i},~~~~\breve{A}_{i}{\not\!p}_{2}~=~-\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{2}{\not\!\!A},~~~~{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{i}~=~-{\not\!\!B}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i},~~~~\breve{B}_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}~=~-\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!\!B}

(247)

where

\breve{A}_{i}\equiv A_{i}-i{\tilde{A}}_{i}\gamma_{5},~~~\breve{B}_{i}\equiv B_{i}-i{\tilde{B}}_{i}\gamma_{5}

(248)

We also use

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA^{i}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}+~A^{i}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}\gamma_{5}~=~-{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{n}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}-{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{n}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA^{i}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{n}+~A^{i}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{n}\gamma_{5}~=~-\breve{A}_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}-\breve{A}_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\gamma_{n}\not{p}_{2}\gamma^{i}\otimes\not{p}_{1}B_{i}~+~\gamma_{n}\not{p}_{2}\gamma^{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\not{p}_{1}\gamma_{5}B_{i}~=~-\not{p}_{2}\otimes\not{p}_{1}\breve{B}_{n}-\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{5}\otimes\not{p}_{1}\breve{B}_{n}\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\gamma^{i}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{n}\otimes\not{p}_{1}B_{i}~+~\gamma^{i}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{n}\gamma_{5}\otimes\not{p}_{1}\gamma_{5}B_{i}~=~-\not{p}_{2}\otimes\breve{B}_{n}\not{p}_{1}-\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{5}\otimes\breve{B}_{n}\not{p}_{1}\gamma_{5}

(249)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{2\over s}\big[{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\otimes B^{i}\gamma_{n}+{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes B^{i}\gamma_{n}\gamma_{5}\big]~=~\gamma_{i}\otimes\gamma_{n}\breve{B}_{i}+\gamma_{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{n}\breve{B}_{i}\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{2\over s}\big[\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{2}{\not\!p}_{1}\otimes B^{i}\gamma_{n}+\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{2}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}\otimes B^{i}\gamma_{n}\gamma_{5}\big]~=~\gamma_{i}\otimes\breve{B}_{i}\gamma_{n}+\gamma_{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\breve{B}_{i}\gamma_{n}\gamma_{5}

11.1.4
Formulas with
\gamma
-matrices and two gluon fields

With definition (
246
), we have the following formulas

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA_{i}\otimes{\tilde{B}}_{j}=g_{ij}{\tilde{A}}_{k}\otimes B^{k}-{\tilde{A}}_{j}\otimes B_{i},~~~\tilde{A}_{i}\otimes B_{j}=g_{ij}A_{k}\otimes{\tilde{B}}^{k}-A_{j}\otimes{\tilde{B}}_{i}

(250)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\tilde{A}_{i}\otimes{\tilde{B}}_{j}=-g_{ij}A_{k}\otimes B^{k}+A_{j}\otimes B_{i},~~~\Rightarrow~~~\tilde{A}_{i}\otimes\tilde{B}^{i}=-A_{i}\otimes B^{i},~~\tilde{A}_{i}\otimes B^{i}=A_{i}\otimes\tilde{B}^{i}

Using these formulas, after some algebra one obtains

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\gamma_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}A^{i}\otimes\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}B^{j}+\gamma_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}A^{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}B^{j}\gamma_{5}={\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{n}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{m}+{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{n}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{m}\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\gamma_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{m}A^{i}\otimes\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}B^{j}+\gamma_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{m}A^{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}B^{j}\gamma_{5}={\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{n}\otimes\breve{B}_{m}{\not\!p}_{1}+{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{n}\gamma_{5}\otimes\breve{B}_{m}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\gamma_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}A^{i}\otimes\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{n}B^{j}+\gamma_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}A^{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{n}B^{j}\gamma_{5}=\breve{A}_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{m}+\breve{A}_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{m}\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\gamma_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{m}A^{i}\otimes\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{n}B^{j}+\gamma_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{m}A^{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{n}B^{j}\gamma_{5}=\breve{A}_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\breve{B}_{m}{\not\!p}_{1}+\breve{A}_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{5}\otimes\breve{B}_{m}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{5}

(251)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{m}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{n}+{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{n}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{m}\gamma_{5}~=~g_{mn}{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{k}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{k}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{m}\otimes\breve{B}_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}+{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{n}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{5}\breve{B}_{m}{\not\!p}_{1}~=~g_{mn}{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{k}\otimes\breve{B}^{k}{\not\!p}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\breve{A}_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{n}+\gamma_{5}\breve{A}_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{m}\gamma_{5}~=~g_{mn}\breve{A}_{k}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{k}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\breve{A}_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\breve{B}_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}+\gamma_{5}\breve{A}_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\gamma_{5}\breve{B}_{m}{\not\!p}_{1}~=~g_{mn}\breve{A}_{k}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\breve{B}^{k}{\not\!p}_{1}

(252)

The corollary of Eq. (
252
) is

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{k}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{k}\gamma_{5}~=~{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{k}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{k},~~~~~~~~{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{k}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{5}\breve{B}^{k}{\not\!p}_{1}~=~{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{k}\otimes\breve{B}^{k}{\not\!p}_{1}

\displaystyle\hskip-31.29802pt\gamma_{5}\breve{A}_{k}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{k}\gamma_{5}~=~\breve{A}_{k}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{k},~~~~~~~~\gamma_{5}\breve{A}_{k}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\gamma_{5}\breve{B}^{k}{\not\!p}_{1}~=~\breve{A}_{k}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\breve{B}^{k}{\not\!p}_{1}

(253)

From Eqs. (
251
) and (
252
) one easily obtains

\gamma_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}A^{i}\otimes\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}B^{j}+\gamma_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}A^{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}B^{j}\gamma_{5}~+~m\leftrightarrow n~=~2g_{mn}{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{k}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{k}

(254)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\gamma_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}A^{i}\otimes\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}B^{j}+\gamma_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}A^{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{i}B^{j}\gamma_{5}~-~m\leftrightarrow n~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~2{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{n}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{m}~-~m\leftrightarrow n,

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\gamma_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{m}A^{i}\otimes\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{n}B^{j}+\gamma_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{m}A^{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma_{n}B^{j}\gamma_{5}~-~m\leftrightarrow n~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~2\breve{A}_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\breve{B}_{m}{\not\!p}_{1}~-~m\leftrightarrow n

(255)

We need also formulas

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{4\over s^{2}}A^{i}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}\otimes B^{j}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt=~A^{i}\gamma_{j}\otimes B^{j}\gamma_{i}-iA^{i}\gamma_{j}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\tilde{B}}^{j}\gamma_{i}+i{\tilde{A}}^{i}\gamma_{j}\otimes B^{j}\gamma_{i}\gamma_{5}+{\tilde{A}}^{i}\gamma_{j}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\tilde{B}}^{j}\gamma_{i}\gamma_{5},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{4\over s^{2}}\big(A^{i}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}\otimes B^{j}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}+A^{i}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{j}\gamma_{5}\otimes B^{j}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\gamma_{5}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt=~\gamma^{j}\breve{A}_{i}\otimes\gamma^{i}\breve{B}_{j}+\gamma^{j}\breve{A}_{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma^{i}\breve{B}_{j}\gamma_{5},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\gamma_{i}\breve{A}_{j}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma_{j}\breve{A}_{i}\gamma_{5}~=~\gamma_{i}\breve{A}_{j}\otimes\gamma^{i}\breve{B}^{j}-\gamma_{i}\breve{A}^{i}\otimes\gamma_{j}\breve{B}^{j}

(256)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA_{k}\gamma_{i}\not{p}_{2}\gamma^{j}\otimes B_{j}\gamma^{i}\not{p}_{1}\gamma^{k}~=~\not{p}_{2}\breve{A}_{i}\otimes\not{p}_{1}\breve{B}^{i}~=~{\not\!\!A}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\otimes{\not\!\!B}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA_{k}\gamma^{j}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\otimes B_{j}\gamma^{k}\not{p}_{1}\gamma^{i}~=~\breve{A}_{i}\not{p}_{2}\otimes\breve{B}_{i}\not{p}_{1}~=~\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}\otimes\gamma^{i}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!\!B},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA_{k}\gamma_{i}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{j}\otimes B^{j}\gamma^{k}\not{p}_{1}\gamma^{i}~=~\not{p}_{2}\breve{A}_{i}\otimes\breve{B}^{i}\not{p}_{1}~=~~{\not\!\!A}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\otimes\gamma^{i}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!\!B},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA_{k}\gamma_{j}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\otimes B^{j}\gamma^{i}\not{p}_{1}\gamma^{k}~=~\breve{A}_{i}\not{p}_{2}\otimes\not{p}_{1}\breve{B}^{i}~=~\gamma_{i}{\not\!p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}\otimes{\not\!\!B}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i},

(257)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA^{k}\gamma_{m}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{j}\otimes B^{j}\gamma_{n}\not{p}_{1}\gamma_{k}+m\leftrightarrow n-g_{mn}A^{k}\gamma_{i}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{j}\otimes B^{j}\gamma^{i}\not{p}_{1}\gamma_{k}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~\breve{A}_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\breve{B}_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}+m\leftrightarrow n-g_{mn}\breve{A}_{k}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes\breve{B}^{k}{\not\!p}_{1},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA^{k}\gamma_{j}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{m}\otimes B^{j}\gamma_{k}\not{p}_{1}\gamma_{n}+m\leftrightarrow n-g_{mn}A^{k}\gamma_{j}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\otimes B^{j}\gamma_{k}\not{p}_{1}\gamma^{i}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{m}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{n}+m\leftrightarrow n-g_{mn}{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{k}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{k},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA^{k}\gamma_{m}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{j}\otimes B^{j}\gamma_{k}\not{p}_{1}\gamma_{n}+m\leftrightarrow n-g_{mn}A^{k}\gamma_{j}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\otimes B^{j}\gamma_{k}\not{p}_{1}\gamma^{i}

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~\breve{A}_{m}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}_{n}+m\leftrightarrow n-g_{mn}\breve{A}_{k}{\not\!p}_{2}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{k},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptA^{k}\gamma_{j}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{m}\otimes B^{j}\gamma_{n}\not{p}_{1}\gamma_{k}+m\leftrightarrow n-g_{mn}A^{k}\gamma_{j}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\otimes B^{j}\gamma_{k}\not{p}_{1}\gamma^{i}~=~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{m}\otimes\breve{B}_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}+m\leftrightarrow n-g_{mn}{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{k}\otimes\breve{B}^{k}{\not\!p}_{1},

(258)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{2\over s}\big[A_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{j}\otimes B_{j}\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}+A_{i}{\not\!p}_{1}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{j}\gamma_{5}\otimes B_{j}\gamma_{\nu_{\perp}}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}\gamma_{5}\big]

(259)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-\gamma_{i}\breve{A}_{n}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{i}-\gamma_{i}\breve{A}_{n}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\not\!p}_{1}\breve{B}^{i}\gamma_{5}~=~\gamma_{i}\breve{A}_{n}\otimes{\not\!\!B}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}+\gamma_{i}\breve{A}_{n}\gamma_{5}\otimes{\not\!\!B}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}\gamma_{5},

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{2\over s}\big[A_{i}\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{j}\otimes B_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}+A_{i}\gamma_{n}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma^{j}\gamma_{5}\otimes B_{j}{\not\!p}_{2}{\not\!p}_{1}\gamma^{i}\gamma_{5}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=-{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{i}\otimes\gamma^{i}\breve{B}_{n}-{\not\!p}_{2}\breve{A}_{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma^{i}\breve{B}_{n}\gamma_{5}~=~{\not\!\!A}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\otimes\gamma^{i}\breve{B}_{n}+{\not\!\!A}{\not\!p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\gamma_{5}\otimes\gamma^{i}\breve{B}_{n}\gamma_{5}.

11.2
Parametrization of leading-twist matrix elements

Let us first consider matrix elements of operators without
\gamma_{5}
. The standard parametrization of quark TMDs reads
(see e.g. Ref.
[
64
]
))

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\gamma^{\mu}\psi_{f}(0)|A\rangle

(260)

\displaystyle\hskip 76.82243pt=~p_{1}^{\mu}f_{1}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})+k_{\perp}^{\mu}f_{\perp}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})+p_{2}^{\mu}{2m^{2}_{N}\over s}f_{3}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\psi_{f}(0)|A\rangle~=~m_{N}e^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

for quark distributions in the projectile and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(0)\gamma^{\mu}\psi_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle

(261)

\displaystyle\hskip 76.82243pt=~-p_{1}^{\mu}{\bar{f}}_{1}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})-k_{\perp}^{\mu}{\bar{f}}_{\perp}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})-p_{2}^{\mu}{2m^{2}_{N}\over s}{\bar{f}}_{3}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(0)\psi_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle~=~m_{N}{\bar{e}}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

for the antiquark distributions.

17
17

17

In an arbitrary gauge, there are gauge links to
-\infty
as displayed in eq. (
37
).

The corresponding matrix elements for the target are obtained by trivial replacements
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
,
x_{\bullet}\leftrightarrow x_{\ast}

and
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta
:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\ast}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\beta x_{\ast}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle B|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})\gamma^{\mu}\psi_{f}(0)|B\rangle

(262)

\displaystyle\hskip 76.82243pt=~p_{2}^{\mu}f_{1}^{f}(\beta,k_{\perp})+k_{\perp}^{\mu}f_{\perp}^{f}(\beta,k_{\perp})+p_{1}^{\mu}{2m^{2}_{N}\over s}f_{3}^{f}(\beta,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\ast}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\beta x_{\ast}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle B|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})\psi_{f}(0)|B\rangle~=~m_{N}e^{f}(\beta,k_{\perp}),

and

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\ast}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\beta x_{\ast}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle B|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(0)\gamma^{\mu}\psi_{f}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})|B\rangle

(263)

\displaystyle\hskip 76.82243pt=~-p_{2}^{\mu}{\bar{f}}_{1}^{f}(\beta,k_{\perp})-k_{\perp}^{\mu}{\bar{f}}_{\perp}^{f}(\beta,k_{\perp})-p_{1}^{\mu}{2m^{2}_{N}\over s}{\bar{f}}_{3}^{f}(\beta,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\ast}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\beta x_{\ast}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle B|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(0)\psi_{f}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})|B\rangle~=~m_{N}{\bar{e}}^{f}(\beta,k_{\perp}).

Matrix elements of operators with
\gamma_{5}
are parametrized as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}\psi_{f}(0)|A\rangle~=~-i\epsilon_{\mu_{\perp}i}k^{i}g^{\perp}_{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(0)\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}\psi_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle~=~-i\epsilon_{\mu_{\perp}i}k^{i}{\bar{g}}^{\perp}_{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

(264)

The corresponding matrix elements for the target are obtained by trivial replacements
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
,
x_{\bullet}\leftrightarrow x_{\ast}

and
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta
similarly to eq. (
263
).

The parametrization of time-odd Boer-Mulders TMDs are

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\sigma^{\mu\nu}\psi_{f}(0)|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt=~{1\over m_{N}}(k_{\perp}^{\mu}p_{1}^{\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu)h_{1f}^{\perp}(\alpha,k_{\perp})+{2m_{N}\over s}(p_{1}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu)h_{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt+~{2m_{N}\over s}(k_{\perp}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu)h_{3f}^{\perp}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(0)\sigma^{\mu\nu}\psi_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt=~-{1\over m_{N}}(k_{\perp}^{\mu}p_{1}^{\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu){\bar{h}}_{1f}^{\perp}(\alpha,k_{\perp})-{2m_{N}\over s}(p_{1}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu){\bar{h}}_{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt-~{2m_{N}\over s}(k_{\perp}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}-\mu\leftrightarrow\nu){\bar{h}}_{3f}^{\perp}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

(265)

and similarly for the target with usual replacements
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
,
x_{\bullet}\leftrightarrow x_{\ast}

and
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta
.

Note that
the coefficients in front of
f_{3}
,
g^{\perp}_{f}
,
h
and
h_{3}^{\perp}
in eqs. (
260
), (
262
), (
264
), and (
265
)
contain an extra
{1\over s}
since
p_{2}^{\mu}
enters only through the direction
of gauge link so the result should not depend on rescaling
p_{2}\rightarrow\lambda p_{2}
. For this reason, these functions do not contribute to
W(q)
in our approximation.

Last but not least, an important point in our analysis is that any
f(x,k_{\perp})
may have only logarithmic dependence on Bjorken
x
but
not the power dependence
\sim{1\over x}
.
Indeed, the low-
x
behavior of TMDs is determined by pomeron exchange with the nucleon. The interaction of TMD with BFKL pomeron
is specified by so-called impact factor and it is easy to check that the impact factors for all leading-twist TMDs are similar and do not give
extra
{1\over x}
factors. The only
{1\over x}
may had come from some unfortunate definition of TMD which includes factor
s
artificially, but
from power counting (
39
) we see that all definitions of leading-twist TMDs do not have such factors.

11.3
Matrix elements of quark-quark-gluon operators

In this section we will demonstrate that matrix elements of quark-antiquark-gluon operators
from section
6
can be expressed in terms of leading-power
matrix elements from section
11.2
.

First, let us note that operators
{1\over\alpha}
and
{1\over\beta}
in Eqs. (
27
) are
replaced by
\pm{1\over\alpha_{q}}
and
\pm{1\over\beta_{q}}
in forward matrix elements. Indeed,

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}}\langle\bar{\Phi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Gamma{1\over\alpha+i\epsilon}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

(266)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~{1\over i}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}\!\int_{-\infty}^{0}\!\!\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}}\langle\bar{\Phi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Gamma\psi(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},0_{\perp})\rangle_{A}=~{1\over\alpha_{q}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}}\langle\bar{\Phi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Gamma\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

where
\bar{\Phi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})
can be
{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})
or
{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})A_{i}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})
and
\Gamma
can be any
\gamma
-matrix.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\big)(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Gamma\Phi(0)\rangle_{A}=~{1\over\alpha_{q}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Gamma\Phi(0)\rangle_{A}

(267)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\big)(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Gamma{1\over\alpha+i\epsilon}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}=~{1\over\alpha_{q}^{2}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}}\langle\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Gamma\psi(0)\rangle_{A}

where
\Phi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})
can be
\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})
or
A_{i}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})
. We need also

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}}\langle\big(\bar{\psi}{1\over\alpha-i\epsilon}\big)(0)\Gamma\Phi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\rangle_{A}=~-{1\over\alpha_{q}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}}\langle\bar{\psi}(0)\Gamma\Phi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\rangle_{A}

\displaystyle\hskip-19.91692pt\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}}\langle\bar{\Phi}(0)\Gamma{1\over\alpha+i\epsilon}\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\rangle_{A}=~-{1\over\alpha_{q}}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}}\langle\bar{\Phi}(0)\Gamma\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\rangle_{A}

(268)

The corresponding formulas for target matrix elements are obtained by substitution
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta
(and
x_{\bullet}\leftrightarrow x_{\ast}
).

Next, we will use QCD equation of motion to reduce quark-quark-gluon TMDs to leading-twist TMDs (see Ref.
[
20
]
).
Let us start with matrix element

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}\breve{A}_{i}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\psi(0)|A\rangle

(269)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|\bar{\psi}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){\not\!\!A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\psi(0)|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}

\displaystyle\hskip 88.20354pt\times~\big[\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{k}_{\perp}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\psi(0)|A\rangle+i\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftarrow}}{{{\not\!\!D}}}_{\perp}\!\gamma_{j}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\psi(0)|A\rangle\big].

Using QCD equations of motion (
15
) we can rewrite the r.h.s. of eq. (
269
) as

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\big[\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{k}_{\perp}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\psi(0)|A\rangle+\alpha_{q}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{1}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\psi(0)|A\rangle\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\Big[-k_{i}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}\psi(0)|A\rangle+~\alpha_{q}{s\over 2}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\gamma_{i}\psi(0)|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07164pt-~i\epsilon_{ij}k^{j}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{5}\psi(0)|A\rangle+i{s\over 2}\alpha\epsilon_{ij}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\gamma^{j}\gamma_{5}\psi(0)|A\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83385pt=~-k_{i}8\pi^{3}sf_{1}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})+8\pi^{3}s\alpha_{q}k_{i}\big[f_{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})+g^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})\big],

(270)

where we used parametrizations (
260
) and (
264
) for the leading power matrix elements.

Now, the second term in eq. (
270
) contains extra
\alpha_{q}
with respect to the first term

18
18

18

As discussed in the end of Sect.
11.2
, all leading-twist TMDs can have only logarithmic dependence on
Bjorken
x
(which is here either
\alpha_{q}
for the projectile or
\beta_{q}
for the target matrix elements).

, so
it should be neglected in our kinematical region
s\gg Q^{2}\gg q_{\perp}^{2}
and we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|\bar{\psi}^{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}\breve{A}_{i}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\psi^{f}(0)|A\rangle

(271)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|\bar{\psi}^{f}{\not\!\!A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{i}\psi^{f}(0)|A\rangle~=~-k_{i}f_{1}^{f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})

By complex conjugation

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\breve{A}_{i}(0)\not{p}_{2}\psi_{f}(0)|A\rangle

(272)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|\bar{\psi}^{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\gamma_{i}\not{p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}\psi^{f}(0)|A\rangle=~-k_{i}f_{1f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}).

For the corresponding antiquark distributions we get

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(0)\breve{A}_{i}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}\psi_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=~{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\Big[-\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{i}\not{p}_{2}\not{k}_{\perp}\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt-~i\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{i}\not{p}_{2}{\not\!\!D}_{\perp}\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle\Big]~=~-k_{i}{\bar{f}}_{1f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})

(273)

and

\hskip 0.0pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(0)\not{p}_{2}\breve{A}_{i}(0)\psi_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle~=~-k_{i}{\bar{f}}_{1f}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}).

(274)

The corresponding target matrix elements are obtained by trivial replacements

x_{\ast}\leftrightarrow x_{\bullet}
,
\alpha_{q}\leftrightarrow\beta_{q}
and

\not{p}_{2}\leftrightarrow\not{p}_{1}
.

Next, let us consider

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\psi(0)|A\rangle

(275)

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt=~{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt\times~\Big[\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{k}_{\perp}\not{p}_{2}\psi(0)|A\rangle+i\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftarrow}}{{{\not\!\!D}}}_{\perp}\not{p}_{2}\psi(0)|A\rangle\Big].

Using QCD equation of motion and parametrization (
265
), one can rewrite the r.h.s. of this equation as

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\Big[\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{k}_{\perp}\not{p}_{2}\psi(0)|A\rangle+\alpha_{q}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{1}\not{p}_{2}\psi(0)|A\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~i{k_{\perp}^{2}\over m_{N}}h_{1}^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})+\alpha_{q}m_{N}\big[e(\alpha,k_{\perp})+ih(\alpha,k_{\perp})\big].

(276)

Again, only the first term contributes in our kinematical region so we finally get

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}^{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\psi^{f}(0)|A\rangle~=~i{k_{\perp}^{2}\over m}h_{1f}^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}).

(277)

By complex conjugation we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}^{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}(0)\psi^{f}(0)|A\rangle~=~i{k_{\perp}^{2}\over m}h_{1f}^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}).

(278)

For corresponding antiquark distributions one gets in a similar way

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}^{f}(0)\not{p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\psi^{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle=~i{k_{\perp}^{2}\over m}{\bar{h}}_{1f}^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}^{f}(0)\not{p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}(0)\psi^{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle=~i{k_{\perp}^{2}\over m}{\bar{h}}_{1f}^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp}).

(279)

The target matrix elements are obtained by usual replacements

x_{\ast}\leftrightarrow x_{\bullet}
,
\alpha_{q}\leftrightarrow\beta_{q}
and

\not{p}_{2}\leftrightarrow\not{p}_{1}
.

Finally, we need

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){\not\!\!A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\not{p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}(0)\psi(0)|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84526pt=~{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Big(\not{k}_{\perp}+i\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftarrow}}{{{\not\!\!D}}}\big)\not{p}_{2}\big(\not{k}_{\perp}-i{{\not\!\!D}}\big)\psi(0)|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt=~{k_{\perp}^{2}\over 16\pi^{3}}f_{1}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})~+~O(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})

(280)

and similarly

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){\not\!\!A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\sigma_{\ast i}{\not\!\!A}(0)\psi(0)|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84526pt=~{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\Big(\not{k}_{\perp}+i\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\leftarrow}}{{{\not\!\!D}}}\big)\sigma_{\ast i}\big(\not{k}_{\perp}-i{{\not\!\!D}}\big)\psi(0)|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84526pt=~{1\over 16\pi^{3}}{k_{i}k_{\perp}^{2}\over m}h_{1}^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})~+~O(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})

(281)

For corresponding antiquark distributions we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(0){\not\!\!A}(0)\not{p}_{2}{\not\!\!A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt=~-{k_{\perp}^{2}\over 16\pi^{3}}{\bar{f}}_{1}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})~+~O(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}dx_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha_{q}x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){\not\!\!A}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\sigma_{\ast i}{\not\!\!A}(0)\psi(0)|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59605pt=~-{1\over 16\pi^{3}}{k_{i}k_{\perp}^{2}\over m}h_{1}^{\perp}(\alpha_{q},k_{\perp})~+~O(\alpha_{q},\beta_{q})

(282)

Also, as we saw in Sect.
6.2.3
, at the leading order in
N_{c}
there is one quark-antiquark-gluon
operator that does not reduce to twist-2 distributions. It can be parametrized as follows (cf. Eq. (
279
))

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}{2\over s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})\big[A_{i}(x)\sigma_{\ast j}-{1\over 2}g_{ij}A^{k}\sigma_{\ast k}(x)\big]\psi_{f}(0)|A\rangle~

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt=~-(k_{i}k_{j}+{1\over 2}g_{ij}k_{\perp}^{2}){1\over m}h_{A}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 16\pi^{3}}{2\over s}\!\int\!dx_{\bullet}d^{2}x_{\perp}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}~\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}_{f}(0)[A_{i}(0)\sigma_{{\bullet}j}-{1\over 2}g_{ij}A^{k}\sigma_{{\bullet}k}(0)]\psi_{f}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle~

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89409pt=~-(k_{i}k_{j}+{1\over 2}g_{ij}k_{\perp}^{2}){1\over m}{\bar{h}}_{A}^{f}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

(283)

and similarly for the target matrix elements.

11.4
Parametrization of TMDs from section
7.1

We parametrize TMDs from section
7.1
as follows

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!d^{2}x_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){2\not{p}_{2}\over s}\big[F_{\ast i}(0)+i\gamma_{5}{\tilde{F}}_{\ast i}(0)\big]\psi(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~k_{i}j_{1}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!d^{2}x_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){2\not{p}_{2}\over s}\big[F_{\ast i}(0)-i\gamma_{5}{\tilde{F}}_{\ast i}(0)\big]\psi(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~k_{i}j_{2}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!d^{2}x_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){2\not{p}_{2}\over s}\big[F_{\ast i}(0)-i\gamma_{5}{\tilde{F}}_{\ast i}(0)\big]\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~k_{i}{\bar{j}}_{1}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!d^{2}x_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\bullet}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}\langle A|{\bar{\psi}}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp}){2\not{p}_{2}\over s}\big[F_{\ast i}(0)+i\gamma_{5}{\tilde{F}}_{\ast i}(0)\big]\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~k_{i}{\bar{j}}_{2}(\alpha,k_{\perp})

(284)

By complex conjugation we get

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!d^{2}x_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{0}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}\langle A|\bar{\psi}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},0_{\perp}){2\not{p}_{2}\over s}[F_{\ast i}(x)-i\gamma_{5}{\tilde{F}}_{\ast i}(x)]\psi(0)|A\rangle,

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~k_{i}j_{1}^{\star}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!d^{2}x_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{0}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}\langle A|\bar{\psi}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},0_{\perp}){2\not{p}_{2}\over s}\big[F_{\ast i}(x)+i\gamma_{5}{\tilde{F}}_{\ast i}(x)\big]\psi(0)|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~k_{i}j_{2}^{\star}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!d^{2}x_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{0}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}\langle A|\bar{\psi}(0){2\not{p}_{2}\over s}[F_{\ast i}(x)+i\gamma_{5}{\tilde{F}}_{\ast i}(x)]\psi(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},0_{\perp})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~k_{i}{\bar{j}}_{1}^{\star}(\alpha,k_{\perp}),

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt{1\over 8\pi^{3}s}\!\int\!d^{2}x_{\perp}dx_{\bullet}~e^{-i\alpha x_{\bullet}+i(k,x)_{\perp}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{0}\!dx^{\prime}_{\bullet}\langle A|\bar{\psi}(0){2\not{p}_{2}\over s}\big[F_{\ast i}(x)-i\gamma_{5}{\tilde{F}}_{\ast i}(x)\big]\psi(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},0_{\perp})|A\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~k_{i}{\bar{j}}_{2}^{\star}(\alpha,k_{\perp}).

(285)

Note that unlike two-quark matrix elements,
quark-quark-gluon ones may have imaginary parts.

Target matrix elements are obtained by usual substitutions

\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta
,
\not{p}_{2}\leftrightarrow\not{p}_{1}
,
x_{\bullet}\leftrightarrow x_{\ast}
, and
{\hat{F}}_{\ast i}\leftrightarrow{\hat{F}}_{{\bullet}i}
.

For completeness let us present the explicit form of the gauge links in an arbitrary gauge:

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt{\bar{\psi}}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp})F_{\ast i}(0)\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp})~\rightarrow~{\bar{\psi}}(x^{\prime}_{\bullet},x_{\perp})[x^{\prime}_{\bullet},-\infty_{\bullet}]_{x}[x_{\perp},0_{\perp}]_{-\infty_{\bullet}}

(286)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times~[-\infty_{\bullet},0]_{0_{\perp}}F_{\ast i}(0)[0,-\infty_{\bullet}]_{0_{\perp}}[0_{\perp},x_{\perp}]_{-\infty_{\bullet}}[-\infty_{\bullet},x_{\bullet}]_{x}\psi(x_{\bullet},x_{\perp}).

11.5
Gluon power corrections from
J_{A}^{\mu}(x)J_{A\mu}(0)
terms

There is one more type of contributions proportional to the product of quark and gluon TMDs

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526ptJ_{A}^{\mu}(x)J_{A}^{\nu}(0)~=~

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~\sum_{\rm flavors}\Big(\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma^{\mu}\psi_{A}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(0)\gamma^{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]+\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma^{\mu}\Xi_{1}(x)\big]\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(0)\gamma^{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt+~\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma^{\mu}\psi_{A}(x)\big]\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(0)\gamma^{\nu}\psi_{A}(0)\big]+\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(x)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(0)\gamma^{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]\Big),

(287)

where we neglected terms which cannot contribute to
W
due to the reason discussed after eq. (
158
), i.e.
that one hadron (“A” or “B”) cannot produce the DY pair on its own.

Let us consider the first term in the r.h.s of this equation

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\check{W}_{\mu\nu}(x)~=~\langle A,B|\big[{\bar{\Xi}}_{1}(x)\gamma_{\mu}\psi_{A}(x)\big]\big[\bar{\psi}_{A}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\Xi_{1}(0)\big]|A,B\rangle

(288)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt=~-{g^{2}\over s(N_{c}^{2}-1)}\langle\big({\bar{\psi}}{1\over\alpha}\big)(x)\gamma_{i}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{\mu}\psi(x){\bar{\psi}}(0)\gamma_{\nu}\not{p}_{2}\gamma_{j}{1\over\alpha}\psi(0)\rangle_{A}\langle A^{ai}(x)A^{aj}(0)\rangle_{B}

To estimate the magnitude of this contribution, first note that

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84526pt\!\int\!dx_{\ast}~e^{-i\beta_{q}x_{\ast}}~\langle B|A^{a}_{i}(x)A^{a}_{j}(0)|B\rangle

(289)

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt=~{4\over s^{2}}\int\!dx_{\ast}~e^{-i\beta_{q}x_{\ast}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{x_{\ast}}\!dx^{\prime}_{\ast}\!\int_{-\infty}^{0}\!dx^{\prime\prime}_{\ast}~\langle B|F^{a}_{{\bullet}i}(x^{\prime}_{\ast},x_{\perp})F^{a}_{{\bullet}j}(x^{\prime\prime}_{\ast},0_{\perp})|B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt=~{4\over\beta_{q}^{2}s^{2}}\!\int\!dx_{\ast}~e^{-i\beta_{q}x_{\ast}}\langle B|F^{a}_{{\bullet}i}(x_{\ast},x_{\perp})F^{a}_{{\bullet}j}(0)|B\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt=~-{1\over\beta_{q}}8\pi^{2}\alpha_{s}\Big[{\cal D}_{g}(\beta_{q},x_{\perp})+{1\over m^{2}}(2\partial_{i}\partial_{j}+g_{ij}\partial_{\perp}^{2}{\cal H}_{g}(\beta_{q},x_{\perp})\big]

where we used parametrization (3.26) from Ref.
[
29
]
. Since the gluon TMDs
{\cal D}_{g}(x_{B},x_{\perp})
and
{\cal H}_{g}(\beta_{q},x_{\perp})
behave
only logarithmically as
x_{B}\rightarrow 0

[
62
]
, the contribution of eq. (
288
) to
W(q)
is of order of

{m_{\perp}^{2}\over\beta_{q}s}\ll{m_{\perp}^{2}\over Q^{2}}
. (As discussed in Ref.
[
29
]
, the projectile TMD in the r.h.s. of eq. (
288
) does
not
give
{1\over\alpha_{q}}
after Fourier transformation). Also, this contribution is
\sim{1\over N_{c}}
with respect to our leading terms.

Similarly, all other terms in eq. (
287
) are either

{m_{\perp}^{2}\over\beta_{q}s}
or
{m_{\perp}^{2}\over\alpha_{q}s}
times
{1\over N_{c}}
so they can be neglected.

19
19

19

It is worth mentioning that if the DY pair is produced in the region of rapidity close to the projectile,
the contribution (
289
) may be the most important since gluon parton densities at small
x_{B}

are larger than the quark ones.
